# Simulator — web lane implementation plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

> **Refreshed 2026-09-18** against the code that now exists: the report lane and the planner are on `main`, the engine lane's `sim/` Go module (`sim/api`, `sim/request`, `sim/adapter`, `sim/enginever`) is real and pins `ENGINE_VERSION = df3b36e4c`, and the data lane emits `data/curated/specs.json` (27 specs), `web/src/lib/sim/specs.ts`, `data/builds/<build>/simdb.bin`, `simconsumes.json`, `spellconst/<class>.json` and `data/curated/apl/<spec>.json`. Tasks 1, 2, 6, 7, 8, 19 and 22 were rewritten against that; Task 23 is new. Re-checked against the engine lane's Task 3 fix round at **`sim-engine` 4166eb0**, which moved the level rule into `api.SimLevel`/`SimRequest.Validate`, gave every summary row a unique integer identity, resolved consumables through the build's `simconsumes.json`, and published the id vocabulary as `sim/request/IDS.md`. See the Rulings table for what changed and why.

**Goal:** Build the simulator's whole browser surface — `/sim`, `/sim/<sim_id>`, `/sim/specs`, the WebAssembly worker pool that runs the engine for free on the player's own machine, the one character model shared with the planner, and the ambient surfaces (live DPS in the planner, the execution column on every ranking) — so that a player pastes an addon string and sees, inside a second, what their character does and why.

**Architecture:** One Svelte island (`sim-island`, a standalone Vite entry built through the existing `islandConfig()` factory, like `planner-island` and `report-island`) mounted on three static Astro shells. The engine is `sim.wasm`, loaded after first paint into a pool of `min(8, hardwareConcurrency)` workers, each holding one instance; iterations are split with `simSplit`, run with `simRun`, and the final result combined with `simCombine`, while a running DPS estimate is pooled numerically from each shard's progress so the number moves without a wasm round-trip. Every message is a JSON string: we build `sim.wasm` from the site's own `sim/` module, so the Go adapter runs inside it and the browser gets a finished `SimResult` whose `summary.Summary` the report components render directly — no second renderer, and compare mode is a two-column layout over the same components.

**Tech Stack:** Astro 7 (static, `build.format: 'file'`), Svelte 5 runes, Tailwind 4 with `web/src/styles/tokens.css`, TypeScript 6, Vitest 5, Playwright 1.63 (`E2E_PORT=4325` in this worktree), Cloudflare Workers (`web/src/worker.ts`), Node 22.12.0. **No new runtime or test dependency is added by this lane.**

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-14-simulator-design.md` (sections 4.1–4.7 and 5.1 are this lane). Binding contract: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-14-simulator-interfaces.md` (the "Web" section is this lane; the rest constrains it) — **read-only from here; an amendment is requested in the finish report, never edited in place.** Supporting: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-13-phase-2-addon-design.md` (the FS1 export string), `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-14-phase-3-interfaces.md` (report data, rankings rows, the Worker's shell routes), `design/DESIGN-SYSTEM.md`.

---

## Global Constraints

Every task's requirements implicitly include this section.

**Toolchain**

- Node 22.12.0. Every shell that runs npm starts with `export PATH=/Users/jh/.nvm/versions/node/v22.12.0/bin:$PATH`. All npm commands run from `web/`.
- Playwright needs `ASTRO_PREVIEW_BACKGROUND=1`; it is already in `web/playwright.config.ts`'s `webServer.env` and must stay.
- **End-to-end tests run on port 4325 and nowhere else.** `playwright.config.ts` reads `E2E_PORT` (default 4321, which another checkout holds), so every Playwright command in this plan is prefixed `E2E_PORT=4325`. A bare `npx playwright test` would collide with the main build's preview server and either hang or assert against the wrong site.
- `FOREVER_DATA=fixture` for every test run. `npm run pretest` already sets it; a bare `npx vitest`/`npx playwright` does not, so prefix it.
- Lighthouse (`npm run lhci`) runs **once**, in Task 22. No other task runs it.
- **Tests are scoped to the files the task touches**: `npx vitest run src/lib/sim/run.test.ts` rather than `npm test`. The full suite runs in Task 22 and in CI.
- **`NO_COLOR=1 npx astro check` must report `0 errors`** after every task that adds or edits a `.ts`, `.astro` or `.svelte` file. It type-checks `.svelte` as well as `.ts`, and CI runs it. Three things it catches that a passing test does not: `Ruleset` in `web/src/lib/characters.ts` is the closed union `normal | pvp | rp | hardcore`, so a fixture character key never says `forever`; `RosterRow.class` is optional, so anything passing it to a `string` parameter needs `?? ''`; and `Summary`'s `mechanics`, `threat_by_target`, `taunts` and `phases` are optional, so a sim summary that omits them is legal and must not be indexed bare.
- **eslint and prettier run over the touched files only**: `npx eslint <paths>` and `npx prettier --write <paths>`, never over the whole tree.
- Vitest's default environment in this repository is Node. Any test file that touches `document`, `window`, `Worker` or a module that reads `document.cookie` (which `requestEnvelope` does on every non-GET) **must** start with the line `// @vitest-environment jsdom`, the way `src/lib/account/api.test.ts` and `src/report-island.test.ts` already do. Without it a POST fails as a network error with status 0 and the test's assertion about the status is silently wrong.
- **The API is not running, and this lane adds no HTTP-mocking library.** `web/src/lib/account/api.ts`'s `requestEnvelope` is the one place a browser call to our API is built, and eight existing test files stub it with `vi.stubGlobal('fetch', …)`. Unit tests here do the same, through one shared helper (`web/src/test-support/sim-api.ts`, Task 2); Playwright uses `page.route`. Do not add `msw` or any other mock-server package: a second mocking idiom in a suite that already has one is exactly the duplication these rules forbid.

**Branch and commits**

- Work only in the `sim-web` worktree at `/Users/jh/code/forever/.worktrees/sim-web`, on branch `sim-web`.
- Every commit ends with exactly one trailer line, and it is this one:
  `Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>`
  It is the plan's, and it stands. If the session running a task carries an attribution reminder naming a different model, that reminder is not this lane's; use the line above.
- Conventional-commit types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `docs`, `test`, `chore`, `perf`, `ci`.
- Never `git stash`, never bypass the git hooks, never amend a commit that already exists.

**Contract values, verbatim**

- `ENGINE_VERSION` is the short commit sha of `wowsims-forever`. It is **`df3b36e4c`** today, and it is generated into `sim/enginever/version.go` by `make engine-pin`. It appears in `web/public/_sim/<ENGINE_VERSION>/sim.wasm` and `sim.js` and in every envelope's `engine_version`. **Never type a sha into a component, a test or a fixture**: import `ENGINE_VERSION` from `web/src/lib/sim/version.ts`. A result produced by a different `ENGINE_VERSION` than the current one is still readable and is **labelled stale in the UI; it is never silently re-run.**
- `sim_id`: 12 lowercase base32 characters (`[a-z2-7]{12}`), the same alphabet as `report_id`. Route `/sim/<sim_id>`.
- `spec_slug`: `<class-slug>-<spec-slug>` in lower kebab, e.g. `warrior-fury`, `mage-frost`. The canonical list is `data/curated/specs.json`, generated by the **data lane** (`python -m pipeline specs`) into `sim/specs/specs.go` and `web/src/lib/sim/specs.ts`. **Nothing hardcodes a spec list, a spec count or a class list anywhere else**, and no task in this plan writes or regenerates `specs.ts`.
- The active data build is `web/src/data/active-build.json`'s `build` field (`1.60.1.69893` today). **Never type a build id**; import it the way `web/src/components/report/ClassIcon.svelte` does.
- `character_key`: `<region>/<ruleset>/<name-slug>`.
- Iterations are `500`, `3000` or `10000` — no other value (`sim/api`'s `ValidIterations`).
- `EncounterSpec` defaults are `sim/api`'s `DefaultEncounter()`: `duration_sec` 180, `variation` 0.2, `targets` 1, `execute_ratio` 0.25, `profile` `""`. Its bounds are `sim/api`'s too: duration 60–480 s, targets 1–10, variation and execute ratio 0–1.
- **The engine simulates level 60 and nothing else.** As of `sim-engine` 4166eb0 that rule lives in exactly one place: `api.SimLevel = 60`, enforced by `api.SimRequest.Validate` (`"character.level must be 60, got 40; the engine simulates no other level"`). `request.ErrUnsupportedLevel` is **gone** — do not reference it. `CharacterSpec.level` is therefore always `60`; **the sim page offers no level control at all**, and a character whose talent spend implies a lower level is simmed at 60 with the points it has, which the strip says in words rather than by sending a level the engine will refuse.
- JSON field names in `web/src/lib/sim/types.ts` are identical to the Go `json:` tags in `sim/api/envelope.go` (read them there; that file is the authority). `SimResult.request` is stored whole — there is no `Raw` field to strip and no field to omit.
- **No protobuf crosses a lane boundary, and this lane never builds one.** `sim.wasm` is built in this repository from the site's `sim/` Go module, so `sim/request` (envelope → `RaidSimRequest`) and `sim/adapter` (`RaidSimResult` → `summary.Summary`) both run inside it. The browser sends a `SimRequest` as JSON and receives a finished `SimResult`, summary included. There is no TypeScript protobuf toolchain in `web/`, no request encoder, and no TypeScript copy of the adapter's mapping table.
- **The wasm exports exactly four functions**, all JSON strings in and out: `simRun(requestJSON, callbackId)`, `simSplit(requestJSON, n)`, `simCombine(resultsJSON)`, `simAbort(callbackId)`. The engine's own thirteen `js.Global().Set` entrypoints are an implementation detail behind them and **must not be called**.
- **Aura types are upper case: `'BUFF'` and `'DEBUFF'`.** That is what `logs/engine/summary` writes for a real fight and what the shared `AuraTable` filters on (`track.type === kind`), so it is the one vocabulary for both pages. The adapter is being changed to match it (engine Task 13, which regenerates its goldens anyway); **the web never lower-cases, upper-cases or otherwise normalises an aura type** — a normaliser here would be a second vocabulary pretending to be one. Every comparison on this lane is against `'BUFF'`/`'DEBUFF'` literally, and Task 2 Step 5 fails the fixture derivation loudly if the copied golden still carries the old lower-case form.
- **The progress payload is settled, not open.** The contract pins it at `Pick<SimResult, 'iterations_run' | 'dps'>`, which `web/src/lib/sim/types.ts` mirrors as `SimProgressUpdate`; engine Task 13 builds `sim/cmd/wasm` to it. No task in this plan asks the engine lane to confirm it, and no task may widen it without a contract amendment.
- **A sim summary identifies a row by an integer and names it by an engine action key.** `sim/adapter` (`identity.go`, 4166eb0) gives every ability, cast and aura a unique `spell_id`: an untagged spell keeps its own client id, and every other action — a tagged or ranked spell, an item, an `other` action, an unknown — takes a derived id from a reserved space at or above **2,000,000**, which no client id reaches. `Summarize` refuses to return a summary with a duplicate key (`ErrDuplicateRow`), because the report components' keyed `{#each}` blocks raise a runtime error on one.
  - **Rows are keyed on `spell_id`; a name join is allowed only for synthetic rows.** Every `{#each}` and every map in this lane keys on `spell_id` (with the actor or target guid, exactly as `summary/damage.go`'s `abilityKey`, `CastTable.svelte` and `AuraTable.svelte` do) — a rendered row is **never** keyed on a name, because a name is display text and two rows sharing one is a Svelte 5 runtime error. The one carve-out is matching a sim row against a **logged fight's** row (compare mode, Task 16): a row whose `spell_id` is at or above `SYNTHETIC_ID_BASE` carries no client id at all, so there is nothing to match on, and those rows alone fall back to the resolved, de-varianted, aliased name. Rows below the base are matched by id and never by name. That is ruling 12, and it stands.
  - The name's grammar is `spell:<id>`, `spell:<id>/<tag>`, `spell:<id>+r<rank>`, `spell:<id>/<tag>+r<rank>`, `item:<id>[…]`, `other:<snake_name>[…]` (the engine's own `OtherAction` name, prefix stripped: `other:attack`, `other:rage_gain`) and `unknown[…]`. Every place this lane shows one goes through `resolveActionName()` (Task 23). **No component may render `ability.name` bare.**
- **Buff and consumable ids come from `sim/request/IDS.md`**, generated from the engine's own protobuf descriptors by `go run ./internal/genids`. A buff id is a protobuf field name in lower snake case (`battle_shout`, `blessing_of_kings`). A consumable id is an enum value name (`elixir_of_the_mongoose`, `flask_of_supreme_power`) **or** a client item id spelled `item:<id>`, resolved against `data/builds/<build>/simconsumes.json`; either may be slot-qualified and an imbue must be (`off_hand_imbue:shadow_oil`, `main_hand_imbue:item:3824`). **Read the list, never type an id from memory**, and never hardcode a second copy of it in `web/`. An id the engine cannot map is an error at the boundary, not a silent drop — **the UI must surface it verbatim**, because a sim that quietly ran without a world buff reports a wrong number and says nothing about why.
- **`simconsumes.json` is a runtime asset of the browser lane.** `request.Build` has no consumable table and rejects `item:<id>`; `request.BuildWith(req, Options{Consumables})` takes one, and the wasm fetches `data/builds/<build>/simconsumes.json` to supply it. Task 23 publishes that file to `public/data/<build>/`.
- `GET /v1/me` returns the premium flag as `user.premium`, so the page can decide whether to offer the server lane at all.
- `POST /v1/sims/run` answers 402 with code `premium_required` when the user is not premium.
- Compare mode reads the fight summary from the report's existing `data_base_url`. **No new API.**
- `web/src/lib/sim/worker.ts` owns the pool; `web/src/lib/sim/run.ts` owns request building and partial-result combination. **Neither imports Astro.**

**Design system** (`design/DESIGN-SYSTEM.md`)

- No new hex literal in any component. Colours come from Tailwind utilities bound to `web/src/styles/tokens.css`: `bg-bg`, `bg-raised`, `bg-card-top`, `border-line`, `border-line-soft`, `border-line-warm`, `border-line-warm-strong`, `text`, `text-strong`, `text-muted`, `text-nav`, `text-gold`, `rounded-panel`, `rounded-control`, `rounded-pill`, `font-display`, `font-mono`. Class and rarity colours go through `classColorVar()` / `rarityClassFor()`, never a literal.
- **Svelte 5 runes, no stores.** State is `$state`/`$derived`/`$props`, the way `web/src/lib/planner/store.svelte.ts` and every component under `web/src/components/report/` already do it. There is no `svelte/store` import anywhere in this repository and this lane adds none.
- Heavy, non-landing views are loaded through `createLazyComponent()` from `web/src/lib/report/lazy-component.svelte.ts`, the way the report island loads Compare, Mechanics, Rankings, Timelines, Events and Queries. That is the existing pattern and the reason `/sim`'s first paint can stay cheap.
- No emoji anywhere in the UI. Icons are inline SVG on a 24px grid, 2px stroke, round caps and joins, `currentColor` or gold.
- Errors are plain text with `role="alert"` in `text-strong`, never a colour. `--color-ember` is the sky horizon only; the faction and class colours are never repurposed.
- Hit targets 44px minimum on phone (`min-h-11` on every button, select, tab and row control).
- Page gutter 18px phone / 48px desktop (`px-[18px] md:px-12`); content `max-w-[1344px]`; section gap 22px phone / 32px desktop (`gap-[22px] md:gap-8`). Every `<main>` on this lane uses the site's one class string:
  `class="mx-auto flex w-full max-w-[1344px] flex-col gap-[22px] pt-4 pb-10 md:gap-8 md:px-12 md:pt-7"`.
- Numbers are `font-mono tabular`.
- **Every user-visible string on this lane lives in `web/src/lib/sim/copy.ts`** and is imported by both the component and its test. No string literals in components, and none in a `lib/sim/*.ts` module either, the way `web/src/lib/planner/rules.ts` holds `messages`. This is not a style preference: a sentence declared in `run.ts` and a second one declared in `copy.ts` for the same state is how a page ends up saying two different things, and it is exactly what the pre-flight scan found. Specifically, and enforced by the tasks named:
  - the run loop's failure sentences are `simCopy.stopped` and `simCopy.failed`; **`run.ts` declares no `RUN_CANCELLED` and no `RUN_FAILED`** (Task 5);
  - the character model's refusals are `simCopy.classMismatch(…)`, `simCopy.unknownRace(…)` and `simCopy.unreachableTalents(…)` (Task 6);
  - the execution column's words are `simCopy.executionHeading` and `simCopy.executionUnscored` (Task 10);
  - the sentence module's are `simCopy.noDamage` and `simCopy.proseNames` (Task 14);
  - compare mode's is `simCopy.compareNoPlayer` (Task 16).
  A module needing one imports `simCopy`; `copy.ts` imports nothing from `lib/sim/` except types, so there is no cycle.

**Budgets**

- **Lighthouse (`web/lighthouserc.json`) applies to all three sim pages.** `/sim.html` and `/sim/specs.html` are held to the site's standard mobile budget: performance ≥ 0.95, LCP ≤ 1600 ms, **total blocking time ≤ 100 ms**, CLS ≤ 0.05. That is the point of loading the engine after first paint and creating the pool on the first run. `/sim/simfixtureab.html` inlines a finished result and mounts the report components at load, exactly as `/reports/<id>` does, so it takes **the report page's budget: performance ≥ 0.90 and total blocking time ≤ 200 ms** — the controller has ruled this and the contract records it, so Task 22 writes it rather than proposing it. It is the only relaxation on this lane; nothing else moves.
- Sim page first paint inside the site's 1.6 s mobile LCP budget. **The wasm loads after first paint** and never blocks it: no module in the shell's critical path may import `web/src/lib/sim/engine.ts`, and the pool is created on the first run, not on mount. Task 22 greps `dist/sim-island.js` for `_sim/` to prove it.
- `sim-island.js` ≤ 90 KB gzipped, enforced by `web/scripts/check-island-size.mjs` (which already carries 60 KB for the planner and 140 KB for the report island, plus Cloudflare's 25 MiB per-file cap). The wasm is not counted; it is a separate immutable asset.
- Quick Sim, 3,000 iterations, 4 workers: first estimate within 1 s, final within 8 s.
- Planner live DPS: 500 iterations, under a second, with its error shown. The planner island's own 60 KB budget must not move: if it grows, the engine has leaked onto the planner's critical path through a static import.

**Phone from the start.** Every component in this plan is written with its phone layout in the same step as its desktop layout, not retrofitted, and **~400 px wide is the width that must work** (Playwright's `mobile` project is a Pixel 7 at 412 px). Task 19 is the audit, not the implementation.

## Parallel groups

Task 1 and Task 2 are the base: both must land before anything else, in that order.

| Group | Tasks | Depends on | Independent of |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A — the engine in the browser** | 3, 4, 5 (ordered within the group) | 1, 2 | B, C, E |
| **B — the character model and the names** | 6, 7, 23 (ordered within the group) | 1, 2 | A, C, E |
| **C — the routes, the island and the Worker** | 8, 9 (ordered within the group) | 1, 2 | A, B, E |
| **E — the execution column** | 10 | 1, 2 | A, B, C, D, F |
| **D — the sim page** | 11, 12, 13, 14, 15, 16, 17, 18, 19 (strictly ordered) | A, B, C | E, F |
| **F — the planner surfaces** | 20, 21 (ordered within the group) | A, B | C, D, E |
| **Final** | 22 | everything | — |

Groups A, B, C and E may run at the same time in four worktrees or four subagents. D is one ordered group because every task in it composes the report components on top of the task before it; F is one ordered group for the same reason. Task 22 runs last, alone.

## Task 1: Pin the engine version and put display names over the data lane's spec list

Everything downstream renders results with the report components and reads the spec list. Both already exist on `main` and in the data lane, so this task no longer merges anything and no longer generates anything: it adds the two small modules that were genuinely missing.

**What changed since the first draft.** The first draft merged `phase-3-web` and wrote `data/curated/specs.json` plus `web/scripts/sync-specs.mjs`. Neither is needed any more:

- the report lane is on `main` (`6e3ab17`), and this worktree is already rebased onto it — `web/src/components/report/`, `web/src/lib/report/`, `web/src/worker.ts`'s shell machinery and `islandConfig()` are all present;

```ts
import { existsSync, readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import path from 'node:path';
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { ENGINE_VERSION, engineAssetUrl, engineLabel, isStale } from './version';

// The engine lane's Go module is not on main yet -- this tree carries only the data lane's
// sim/specs -- so the pin assertion runs when the file lands and reports as skipped until
// then. A skip that names its own condition is honest; a test that quietly passes on a
// missing file is the thing this assertion exists to prevent.
const ENGINEVER_GO = path.resolve(import.meta.dirname, '../../../../sim/enginever/version.go');
const enginePinned = existsSync(ENGINEVER_GO);

describe('ENGINE_VERSION', () => {
  it('is a short git sha', () => {
    expect(ENGINE_VERSION).toMatch(/^[0-9a-f]{7,12}$/);
  });

  it.skipIf(!enginePinned)(
    'is the same sha sim/enginever/version.go carries, because make engine-pin writes both',
    () => {
      const pinned = /const Version = "([0-9a-f]{7,12})"/.exec(readFileSync(ENGINEVER_GO, 'utf8'));
      expect(pinned).not.toBeNull();
      expect(ENGINE_VERSION).toBe(pinned![1]);
    },
  );
});

describe('engineAssetUrl', () => {
  it('addresses the immutable per-version directory', () => {
    expect(engineAssetUrl('sim.wasm')).toBe(`/_sim/${ENGINE_VERSION}/sim.wasm`);
    expect(engineAssetUrl('sim.js')).toBe(`/_sim/${ENGINE_VERSION}/sim.js`);
  });

  it('can address an older version, so a stored result stays readable', () => {
    expect(engineAssetUrl('sim.wasm', '6a1c2d9')).toBe('/_sim/6a1c2d9/sim.wasm');
  });
});

describe('isStale', () => {
  it('is false for the current engine and true for any other', () => {
    expect(isStale(ENGINE_VERSION)).toBe(false);
    expect(isStale('6a1c2d9')).toBe(true);
  });

  it('treats a missing version as stale rather than current', () => {
    expect(isStale('')).toBe(true);
  });
});

describe('engineLabel', () => {
  it('reads as a version, not as a hash', () => {
    expect(engineLabel('6a1c2d9')).toBe('Engine 6a1c2d9');
  });
});
```

The cross-file assertion is the point of this test: `make engine-pin` writes `sim/enginever/version.go` and a human writes `version.ts`, so the one way they drift is silently, and the one way to catch it is here.

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the module**

Create `web/src/lib/sim/version.ts`:

```ts
// web/src/lib/sim/version.ts
// One string identifies the engine build everywhere (simulator interface contract, "Engine
// version"): the short sha of wowsims-forever the artifacts were built from. It names the
// immutable asset directory, it goes into every SimRequest and SimResult, and a result that
// carries a different one is labelled stale and never silently re-run.
//
// It is a literal rather than an import from sim/enginever/version.go because the web build
// has no Go toolchain; `make engine-pin` writes both, version.test.ts asserts they agree,
// and web.yml runs that test.

export const ENGINE_VERSION = 'df3b36e4c';

/** Where a version's browser artifacts live. Cached immutably, so the path carries the sha. */
export function engineAssetUrl(file: 'sim.wasm' | 'sim.js', version: string = ENGINE_VERSION): string {
  return `/_sim/${version}/${file}`;
}

/**
 * True when a stored result was produced by some other engine build. An empty version counts
 * as stale: a result with no provenance is exactly the one a player should be told about.
 */
export function isStale(resultVersion: string): boolean {
  return resultVersion !== ENGINE_VERSION;
}

/** How a version reads in the UI. */
export function engineLabel(version: string): string {
  return `Engine ${version}`;
}
```

import { readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';

import { SPECS } from './specs';
import { classOfSpec, dpsSpecs, specLabel, specRow } from './spec-label';

describe('the generated spec list', () => {

    expect(JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(SPECS))).toEqual(curated);

describe('specLabel', () => {

    expect(specLabel('warrior-fury')).toBe('Fury Warrior');
    expect(specLabel('mage-frost')).toBe('Frost Mage');

Create `web/src/lib/sim/spec-label.ts`:

```ts
// web/src/lib/sim/spec-label.ts
// Display names over the data lane's generated spec list.
//
// specs.ts is generated by `python -m pipeline specs` from data/curated/specs.json and must
// never be edited here: the contract makes that file the canonical spec list and forbids a
// second one anywhere. Its `name` is the bare spec -- "Fury", "Frost", "Beast Mastery" --
// because that is what a talent tree is called in the client. A sentence needs the class
// too, and the class's display name is already on classRows (data/builds/<build>/
// classes.json, which lib/planner/reference.ts imports for the planner), so this joins the
// two rather than carrying yet another hardcoded table of class names.
import { classRows } from '../planner/reference';
import { SPEC_BY_KEY, SPECS, type Spec } from './specs';

export function specRow(spec: string): Spec | null {
  return SPEC_BY_KEY[spec] ?? null;
}

/**
 * "Fury Warrior". The spec's own name, then its class's, which is how every player and
 * every guide says it. An unknown slug is returned unchanged rather than guessed at: a spec
 * the site has not heard of yet must still be printable.
 */
export function specLabel(spec: string): string {
  const row = specRow(spec);
  if (row === null) return spec;
  const className = classRows.find((entry) => entry.slug === row.class_slug)?.name;
  return className === undefined ? row.name : `${row.name} ${className}`;
}

/** The class slug, for classColorVar and the planner. Empty for an unknown spec. */
export function classOfSpec(spec: string): string {
  return specRow(spec)?.class_slug ?? '';
}

/**
 * The specs the simulator offers. Launch scope is Quick Sim for damage specs (design,
 * "Scope at launch"): tanks and healers are research problems and stay out of the first
 * cut, so they are filtered here rather than being absent from the canonical list.
 */
export function dpsSpecs(): readonly Spec[] {
  return SPECS.filter((row) => row.role === 'dps');
}
```

## Task 2: The envelope types, the sims API client, and the fetch stub

The contract's JSON shapes, one client over them, and one `vi.stubGlobal('fetch')` route table that answers every route the contract defines. The API is not running, so this is what every other task tests against.

**What changed since the first draft.** The first draft added `msw` as a devDependency and a Mock Service Worker server. This repository already has one idiom for this — eight test files (`src/lib/account/api.test.ts`, `src/lib/rankings/api.test.ts`, `src/lib/upload/multipart.test.ts`, `src/lib/report/load.test.ts`, `src/lib/report/percentile.test.ts`, `src/lib/planner/share.test.ts`, `src/worker.test.ts` and `src/lib/sim/api.test.ts` once this task lands) stub `globalThis.fetch` with `vi.stubGlobal` and build responses with `new Response(JSON.stringify(envelope), …)`. A second mocking library beside it would be a second thing to learn, a second thing to keep in step with `requestEnvelope`, and a dependency this lane does not need. `web/src/test-support/sim-api.ts` is the same idiom, factored once.

Every one of these is consumed by name later: `SimProgressUpdate` by Tasks 3, 4, 5, 11 and 20; `IterationCount` by Task 13; `GearSlot` and `CharacterSpec` by Task 6.

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the contract's types**

Create `web/src/lib/sim/types.ts`:

```ts
// web/src/lib/sim/types.ts
// TypeScript mirrors of sim/api/envelope.go. Every key here is that file's `json:` tag;
// the contract (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-14-simulator-interfaces.md) makes the JSON
// names authoritative and identical in both, so anything renamed here is renamed there.
//
// There is no `raw` and no protobuf here. The contract's rule is that no protobuf crosses a
// lane boundary: `CharacterSpec` is plain JSON, and Go turns it into the engine's
// RaidSimRequest inside our own sim.wasm. Nothing outside Go ever encodes or decodes one.
import type { Summary } from '../report/types';

/** Where the character came from. */
export type SourceKind = 'armory' | 'addon' | 'build' | 'fight' | 'manual';

/** The only iteration counts the contract allows. */
export const ITERATIONS = { live: 500, normal: 3000, precise: 10_000 } as const;
export type IterationCount = (typeof ITERATIONS)[keyof typeof ITERATIONS];

export interface CharacterSource {
  kind: SourceKind;
  /** character_key | "" | build id | "<report_id>:<fight_index>". */
  ref: string;
  /** RFC3339. */
  captured_at: string;
}

export interface GearSlot {
  /** The planner's slot names: head, neck, shoulder, … , ranged. */
  slot: string;
  item_id: number;
  enchant?: number;
  suffix?: number;
}

/**
 * Everything the engine needs about the player, in JSON. `sim/request` turns it into the
 * engine's RaidSimRequest inside our own wasm; nothing outside Go touches a protobuf.
 */
export interface CharacterSpec {
  name: string;
  /** race slug. */
  race: string;
  /** class slug. */
  class: string;
  level: number;
  /** The engine's talents string, e.g. "01102123133-12312312-". */
  talents: string;
  gear: GearSlot[];
  /** Buff ids from the settings bar. */
  buffs: string[];
  consumes: string[];
  professions?: string[];
}

export interface EncounterSpec {
  duration_sec: number;
  variation: number;
  targets: number;
  execute_ratio: number;
  /** "" | "patchwerk" | "encounter:<encounter_id>". */
  profile: string;
}

export const DEFAULT_ENCOUNTER: EncounterSpec = {
  duration_sec: 180,
  variation: 0.2,
  targets: 1,
  execute_ratio: 0.25,
  profile: '',
};

export interface SimRequest {
  engine_version: string;
  /** spec_slug. */
  spec: string;
  source: CharacterSource;
  character: CharacterSpec;
  encounter: EncounterSpec;
  iterations: number;
  /** 0 means random; paired runs set it. */
  random_seed: number;
}

export interface Estimate {
  mean: number;
  stddev: number;
  /** Standard error of the mean. */
  error: number;
  min: number;
  max: number;
}

/**
 * What `simRun` reports through its progress callback: the two fields of a `SimResult` the
 * pool needs to pool an estimate. Not a new shape — confirm it with the engine lane.
 */
export type SimProgressUpdate = Pick<SimResult, 'iterations_run' | 'dps'>;

export interface SimResult {
  sim_id?: string;
  engine_version: string;
  request: SimRequest;
  lane: 'browser' | 'server';
  dps: Estimate;
  iterations_run: number;
  /** Wall clock of the run. */
  duration_ms: number;
  summary: Summary;
  error?: string;
}

/** One row of GET /v1/sims?mine=1. */
export interface SimListRow {
  sim_id: string;
  spec: string;
  dps: number;
  engine_version: string;
  created_at: string;
  title: string;
}

export interface SimListPage {
  rows: SimListRow[];
  total: number;
  page: number;
  per_page: number;
}

export interface SimProgress {
  state: 'queued' | 'running' | 'done' | 'error';
  iterations_done: number;
  dps?: number;
}

export type SpecState = 'validated' | 'in_progress' | 'unsupported';

export interface SpecFidelity {
  spec: string;
  state: SpecState;
  /** Median DPS gap as a fraction; null until the validation job has run. */
  median_gap: number | null;
  parses: number;
  worst_actions: { name: string; sim_casts: number; actual_casts: number }[];
  engine_version: string | null;
  updated_at: string;
}

/** GET /v1/characters/{character_key}/sim-input. */
export interface SimInput {
  spec: string;
  /** Slot name to item id, the planner's own Gear shape. */
  gear: Record<string, number>;
  /** Talent ids in the order the points were spent. */
  talents: number[];
  /**
   * Buff ids, in the engine's own vocabulary (`sim/request/IDS.md`) -- NOT spell ids. The
   * amended contract puts the mapping on the API side: "the web never maps spell ids
   * itself". So these travel into `CharacterSpec.buffs` unchanged, and an id the engine
   * refuses surfaces as the engine's own error rather than being silently dropped.
   */
  buffs: string[];
  /**
   * The character's race slug, when the API has one. The contract's `sim-input` row does
   * not list it and nothing records a race today: an addon export carries one in its FS1
   * string, a combat log does not, and there is no Armory. It is optional here rather than
   * absent because a race is not guessable -- Forever's racials are two actives and two
   * passives each -- so `fromStoredCharacter` refuses rather than substituting one, and
   * the day the companion records it this field is the whole change. See the Rulings
   * table, "the stored character has no race".
   */
  race?: string;
  captured_at: string;
  /**
   * Which source the API actually had. Today that is `"addon"` or `"fight"`, newest wins:
   * nothing in the repo stores an Armory refresh yet, so `"armory"` appears here only when
   * Blizzard's profile API for Forever exists. The shape does not change when it does.
   */
  source: SourceKind;
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the encounter vocabulary**

Create `web/src/lib/sim/encounter.ts`:

```ts
// web/src/lib/sim/encounter.ts
// How an EncounterSpec reads. Two functions, in the base task rather than beside the
// settings bar, because two groups of this plan need them at the same time: the Worker's
// unfurl copy (the routes group) and the settings bar (the character-model group). Pure
// formatting over the shape types.ts defines, so this is where they belong regardless.
import type { EncounterSpec } from './types';

/** Seconds as a clock: 180 is "3:00", not "180s" and not "3 minutes". */
export function durationLabel(seconds: number): string {
  const minutes = Math.floor(seconds / 60);
  return `${minutes}:${String(seconds % 60).padStart(2, '0')}`;
}

/**
 * The settings clause a saved sim's title and a shared sim's unfurl both carry:
 * "raid-buffed, 3:00, single target".
 */
export function encounterLabel(encounter: EncounterSpec, buffed: boolean): string {
  const targets = encounter.targets === 1 ? 'single target' : `${encounter.targets} targets`;
  return `${buffed ? 'raid-buffed' : 'solo'}, ${durationLabel(encounter.duration_sec)}, ${targets}`;
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Start the copy module**

Create `web/src/lib/sim/copy.ts`:

```ts
// web/src/lib/sim/copy.ts
// Every user-visible string on the simulator lane. Components import from here and tests
// assert against the same constants, the way web/src/lib/planner/rules.ts holds `messages`:
// a copy change is then one diff in one file, and no test asserts on a literal that a
// component could quietly stop rendering.
//
// Voice, per design/DESIGN-SYSTEM.md: reference, not pitch. State the number and stop.

export const simCopy = {
  /** Network and API failures. */
  saveFailed: 'The sim could not be saved; try again.',
  loadFailed: 'That sim did not load.',
  notFound: 'No sim with that id.',
  premiumRequired: 'Running on our servers is a premium feature. The browser lane is free and unlimited.',
  specsFailed: 'Spec support could not be read.',
  characterFailed: 'That character could not be read.',

  /**
   * The run loop's two outcomes. They start here, in the base task, rather than in
   * `run.ts`: Task 5 raises them, Task 11's store stores them and Task 13's e2e asserts
   * them, and a copy of either in `run.ts` is how the button and the alert end up saying
   * two different things. `run.ts` declares no message constants of its own.
   */
  stopped: 'Stopped.',
  failed: 'The engine could not run this character.',

  // Used by the sim page (group D) and the planner's live estimate (group F), which run in
  // parallel. Both need the same sentence, so it starts here rather than in either.
  specUnsupportedLead: 'The simulator does not model this spec yet.',
} as const;
```

Later tasks append to this object and never introduce a literal. For reference, the keys they add and where: Task 6 `classMismatch`, `unknownRace`, `unreachableTalents`; Task 7 the source messages; Task 10 `executionHeading`, `executionUnscored`, `executionTitle`; Task 11 `noCharacter` and the switcher copy; Task 12 the settings footnotes; Task 13 `engineLoading`, `precisionNote`, `staleEngine`; Task 14 `noDamage` and `proseNames`; Task 15 the spec-card vocabulary; Task 16 `compareNoPlayer` and `compareFootnote`; Task 17 the save copy; Task 18 the landing copy; Task 21 the card copy.

- [ ] **Step 5: Derive the fixture result from the engine lane's golden**

The fake engine, the fetch stub, the saved-sim page and every results test read one checked-in result, so there is one shape to keep honest.

**Do not hand-write the summary.** The engine lane's `sim/adapter` already produces one, and it has two properties a hand-written fixture would get wrong and no test would catch:

- **abilities and casts are identified by a derived integer and named by an engine action key, not by a display name.** `sim/adapter` emits `spell:25286` at spell id 25286, `spell:25286/1` at 10002025286, `item:14554` at 12014554 and `other:attack/2` at 20022000007 — the derived ids come from the reserved space at or above 2,000,000 so no two rows share a key. A fixture full of "Bloodthirst" at id 0 would make every results test pass against a shape the real engine never sends, and would hide the duplicate-key crash Svelte 5 raises on repeated `{#each}` keys.
- **`Actor.series` is empty and `activity_pct` is 100, not 1.** A sim has no per-second damage series (the numbers are averaged over thousands of iterations), which is exactly why Task 14 mounts `DpsDistribution` instead of `TimeChart`.

So the fixture's `summary` is the engine lane's checked-in golden, copied verbatim:

Two more checks, both properties the report components depend on. First, the aura vocabulary:

```bash
export PATH=/Users/jh/.nvm/versions/node/v22.12.0/bin:$PATH
cd /Users/jh/code/forever/.worktrees/sim-web/web
python3 -c "

kinds = sorted({t['type'] for t in s['auras']})
print('aura types:', kinds)
if any(k not in ('BUFF', 'DEBUFF') for k in kinds):
    sys.exit('aura types are not BUFF/DEBUFF: the engine lane Task 13 aura-case fix has not landed')

```

Expected: `aura types: ['BUFF']`. **If it exits with that message, stop.** `AuraTable` filters `track.type === kind` with `kind` of `'BUFF'` or `'DEBUFF'`, so a lower-case type renders an empty Buffs tab, an empty Debuffs tab, and a `solo` unfurl on a raid-buffed sim — three silent wrongs. The fix is the engine lane's (`sim/adapter/adapter.go` writes the type; Task 13 regenerates the goldens), not a normaliser here. Report it and wait for the regenerated golden; do not hand-edit the copied summary and do not add a case fold anywhere in `web/`.

And second, the row keys:

```bash
python3 -c "
import json
s = json.load(open('src/fixtures/sim/result.json'))['summary']
keys = [(a['guid'], ab.get('via',''), ab['spell_id']) for a in s['damage_done'] for ab in a['abilities']]
keys += [('cast', c['guid'], c['spell_id']) for c in s['casts']]
keys += [('aura', t['target_guid'], t['spell_id']) for t in s['auras']]
assert len(keys) == len(set(keys)), 'duplicate row key'
print('row keys unique:', len(keys))
"
```

Expected: no assertion. A duplicate here is a Svelte 5 runtime error in `ActorRow`, `CastTable` or `AuraTable` the moment the results mount, and `sim/adapter.Summarize` already refuses to produce one (`ErrDuplicateRow`) — so a failure means the fixture was edited by hand.

If the engine lane's golden has moved, these change with it — re-run the copy, re-read the values, and use the new ones in the tests below. **The golden is the engine lane's to change; this lane follows it and never edits the copied summary by hand.** The one field this task does rewrite is `summary.engine_version`, from the golden's placeholder `sim:golden` to `sim:<ENGINE_VERSION>`, because the staleness check is a real behaviour of the page.

`dps.mean` is set to the roster row's own DPS (1130.61 today) so the fixture is internally consistent: a saved sim that showed one number beside a summary totalling another would be a fixture that could never be right. **Read the number out of the golden rather than copying it from here**, and use `fixtureResult.dps.mean` in every test instead of a literal.

The three `engine_version` literals in `result.json` and the two in `specs.json` are the one place a sha is written outside `version.ts`, because a JSON fixture cannot import. Add this guard to `web/src/lib/sim/version.test.ts` so `make engine-pin` cannot move the pin and leave the fixtures behind:

```ts
import { fixtureResult, fixtureSpecs } from '../../test-support/sim-api';

describe('the fixtures', () => {
  it('are pinned to the current engine, since JSON cannot import the constant', () => {
    expect(fixtureResult.engine_version).toBe(ENGINE_VERSION);
    expect(fixtureResult.request.engine_version).toBe(ENGINE_VERSION);
    expect(fixtureResult.summary.engine_version).toBe(`sim:${ENGINE_VERSION}`);
    for (const row of fixtureSpecs) {
      if (row.engine_version !== null) expect(row.engine_version).toBe(ENGINE_VERSION);
    }
  });
});
```

It lives in `version.test.ts` rather than beside the fixtures because `version.ts` is the thing it is guarding, and because Task 1 wrote that file already — this is the one line Task 2 adds to it.

- [ ] **Step 7: Write the failing test for the client**

Create `web/src/lib/sim/api.test.ts`. The jsdom pragma is load-bearing: `requestEnvelope` reads `document.cookie` for the CSRF header on every non-GET, and under the default Node environment that throws before the fetch, which surfaces as a status-0 network failure rather than the 201 the stub sends.

```ts
// @vitest-environment jsdom
import { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import type { CharacterPath } from '../characters';
import { FIXTURE_SIM_ID, TEST_API, createSimApi, fixtureResult } from '../../test-support/sim-api';
import {
  PREMIUM_REQUIRED_STATUS,
  SimApiError,
  dispatchServerSim,
  fetchSim,
  fetchSimInput,
  fetchSimProgress,
  fetchSpecs,
  listMySims,
  saveSim,
} from './api';
import { simCopy } from './copy';
import { ENGINE_VERSION } from './version';

const api = createSimApi();

beforeEach(() => api.install());
afterEach(() => api.reset());

describe('fetchSim', () => {
  it('reads a stored result', async () => {
    const result = await fetchSim(FIXTURE_SIM_ID, TEST_API);
    expect(result.sim_id).toBe(FIXTURE_SIM_ID);
    expect(result.dps.mean).toBeCloseTo(fixtureResult.dps.mean, 5);
    expect(result.summary.damage_done[0].guid).toBe('sim-player');
  });

  it('says so when there is no such sim', async () => {
    await expect(fetchSim('zzzzzzzzzzzz', TEST_API)).rejects.toThrow(simCopy.notFound);
  });
});

describe('saveSim', () => {
  it('posts a browser result and returns the new id', async () => {
    const saved = await saveSim({ ...fixtureResult, sim_id: undefined }, TEST_API);
    expect(saved).toMatch(/^[a-z2-7]{12}$/);
  });

  it('sends the whole request, character included, and never a protobuf', async () => {
    await saveSim({ ...fixtureResult, sim_id: undefined }, TEST_API);
    const sent = api.lastBody() as { request: { character: { talents: string; gear: unknown[] } } };
    expect(sent.request.character.talents).toBe('-5530515-');
    expect(sent.request.character.gear[0]).toEqual({ slot: 'head', item_id: 12640 });
    expect('raw' in sent.request).toBe(false);
  });

  it('sends the CSRF header requestEnvelope reads off the cookie', async () => {
    document.cookie = 'fs_csrf=tok123; path=/';
    await saveSim({ ...fixtureResult, sim_id: undefined }, TEST_API);
    expect(api.lastHeaders()?.get('x-csrf-token')).toBe('tok123');
  });
});

describe('listMySims', () => {
  it('reads the signed-in player’s history', async () => {
    const page = await listMySims(1, TEST_API);
    expect(page.per_page).toBe(100);
    expect(page.rows[0].spec).toBe('warrior-fury');
  });
});

describe('dispatchServerSim', () => {
  it('returns the id the premium lane will write to', async () => {
    const id = await dispatchServerSim(fixtureResult.request, TEST_API);
    expect(id).toMatch(/^[a-z2-7]{12}$/);
  });

  it('turns a 402 into the premium message, not a generic failure', async () => {
    api.setPremium(false);
    const error = await dispatchServerSim(fixtureResult.request, TEST_API).catch((e: unknown) => e);
    expect(error).toBeInstanceOf(SimApiError);
    expect((error as SimApiError).status).toBe(PREMIUM_REQUIRED_STATUS);
    expect((error as SimApiError).message).toBe(simCopy.premiumRequired);
  });
});

describe('fetchSimProgress', () => {
  it('reads the running job’s state', async () => {
    const progress = await fetchSimProgress(FIXTURE_SIM_ID, TEST_API);
    expect(progress.state).toBe('running');
    expect(progress.iterations_done).toBe(4200);
  });
});

describe('fetchSpecs', () => {
  it('reads one row per spec with its fidelity', async () => {
    const specs = await fetchSpecs(TEST_API);
    expect(specs.find((row) => row.spec === 'warrior-fury')?.state).toBe('validated');
    expect(specs.find((row) => row.spec === 'rogue-combat')?.median_gap).toBeNull();
  });
});

describe('fetchSimInput', () => {
  // `normal`, not `forever`: Ruleset is a closed union in web/src/lib/characters.ts --
  // normal | pvp | rp | hardcore -- and anything else is a compile error, not a test detail.
  const path: CharacterPath = { region: 'us', ruleset: 'normal', slug: 'thrallgar' };

  it('reads the newest character model the API holds', async () => {
    const input = await fetchSimInput(path, TEST_API);
    expect(input.spec).toBe('warrior-fury');
    expect(input.gear.head).toBe(12640);
    // Buff ids, not spell ids: the amended contract puts the mapping on the API side.
    expect(input.buffs).toEqual(['battle_shout', 'blessing_of_kings']);
    // Armory is not a source yet; the API answers from the addon export or the last fight.
    expect(input.source).toBe('addon');
  });

  it('spells the route as three path segments, not one encoded key', async () => {
    await fetchSimInput(path, TEST_API);
    expect(api.lastUrl()).toContain('/v1/characters/us/normal/thrallgar/sim-input');
    expect(api.lastUrl()).not.toContain('%2F');
  });
});

describe('engine version', () => {
  it('is what a dispatched request carries', async () => {
    await dispatchServerSim({ ...fixtureResult.request, engine_version: ENGINE_VERSION }, TEST_API);
    const sent = api.lastBody() as { engine_version: string };
    expect(sent.engine_version).toBe(ENGINE_VERSION);
  });
});

describe('the stub itself', () => {
  it('fails loudly on a route nobody wrote, rather than hanging on a real network call', async () => {
    await expect(fetch(`${TEST_API}/v1/nothing`)).rejects.toThrow('unhandled request');
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 9: Write the fetch stub**

Create `web/src/test-support/sim-api.ts`:

```ts
// web/src/test-support/sim-api.ts
// The API does not run in this repository's tests, so every route the simulator contract
// defines is answered here instead.
//
// This is the idiom the repository already has -- vi.stubGlobal('fetch', …) returning a
// real Response, as src/lib/account/api.test.ts and src/lib/rankings/api.test.ts do -- with
// the route table factored out so a route that changes shape changes in one place. An
// unmatched request throws rather than falling through to the network: a test that calls a
// route nobody wrote a handler for must fail loudly, not hang.
//
// lastBody(), lastUrl() and lastHeaders() exist because the assertions that matter are
// often about what the client *sent*: that the character travelled, that a character key
// was path-escaped rather than encoded, that the CSRF header was set, that the engine
// version travelled. Recording them here keeps every test from re-wrapping fetch.
import { vi } from 'vitest';
import fixtureResultJson from '../fixtures/sim/result.json';
import fixtureSpecsJson from '../fixtures/sim/specs.json';
import type { SimResult, SpecFidelity } from '../lib/sim/types';

// Twelve characters of [a-z2-7], because that is what a sim_id is -- the same alphabet
// and length as a report_id. It is not decoration: Task 22's Lighthouse entry for the
// saved-sim page matches on /sim/[a-z2-7]{12}\.html, and an id with a digit outside the
// alphabet silently falls into the catch-all at the wrong budget instead.
export const FIXTURE_SIM_ID = 'simfixtureab';
export const NEW_SIM_ID = 'simnew234567';
export const TEST_API = 'https://api.test';

export const fixtureResult = fixtureResultJson as unknown as SimResult;
export const fixtureSpecs = fixtureSpecsJson as unknown as SpecFidelity[];

/** One route: the method, a pattern over the path, and what it answers. */
export interface StubRoute {
  method: string;
  pattern: RegExp;
  respond: (match: RegExpExecArray, request: Request) => Response | Promise<Response>;
}

export interface SimApiStub {
  /** Installs the fetch stub. Call from `beforeEach`. */
  install(): void;
  /** Removes it and clears every recording. Call from `afterEach`. */
  reset(): void;
  /** Flips the premium flag POST /v1/sims/run checks. Reset by `reset()`. */
  setPremium(value: boolean): void;
  /** Adds a route ahead of the built-in ones, for a test that needs a different answer. */
  route(route: StubRoute): void;
  lastUrl(): string;
  lastBody(): unknown;
  lastHeaders(): Headers | null;
}

/** The Phase 0 envelope every route of ours answers in. */
export function envelope(data: unknown, status = 200): Response {
  return new Response(JSON.stringify({ ok: status < 400, data, error: null, request_id: 'req-test' }), {
    status,
    headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json' },
  });
}

export function failure(message: string, status: number): Response {
  return new Response(JSON.stringify({ ok: false, data: null, error: { message }, request_id: 'req-test' }), {
    status,
    headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json' },
  });
}

export function createSimApi(): SimApiStub {
  let premium = true;
  let lastUrl = '';
  let lastBody: unknown = null;
  let lastHeaders: Headers | null = null;
  let extra: StubRoute[] = [];

  const builtIn: StubRoute[] = [
    {
      method: 'POST',
      pattern: /\/v1\/sims$/,
      respond: () => envelope({ sim_id: NEW_SIM_ID }, 201),
    },
    {
      method: 'POST',
      pattern: /\/v1\/sims\/run$/,
      respond: () => (premium ? envelope({ sim_id: NEW_SIM_ID }, 202) : failure('premium required', 402)),
    },
    {
      method: 'GET',
      pattern: /\/v1\/sims\/([a-z2-7]{12})\/progress$/,
      respond: () => envelope({ state: 'running', iterations_done: 4200, dps: 1559.7 }),
    },
    {
      method: 'GET',
      pattern: /\/v1\/sims\/([a-z2-7]{12})$/,
      respond: (match) =>
        match[1] === FIXTURE_SIM_ID ? envelope(fixtureResult) : failure('no such sim', 404),
    },
    {
      method: 'GET',
      pattern: /\/v1\/sims(\?|$)/,
      respond: () =>
        envelope({
          rows: [
            {
              sim_id: FIXTURE_SIM_ID,
              spec: 'warrior-fury',
              dps: fixtureResult.dps.mean,
              engine_version: fixtureResult.engine_version,
              created_at: '2026-09-14T10:02:00Z',
              title: 'Raid-buffed, 3:00, single target',
            },
          ],
          total: 1,
          page: 1,
          per_page: 100,
        }),
    },
    {
      method: 'GET',
      pattern: /\/v1\/specs$/,
      respond: () => envelope({ specs: fixtureSpecs }),
    },
    // Three path segments, not one key: the contract spells this route the way the existing
    // character route is spelled. `source` is "addon" or "fight" -- Armory is not a source yet.
    {
      method: 'GET',
      pattern: /\/v1\/characters\/[^/]+\/[^/]+\/[^/]+\/sim-input$/,
      respond: () =>
        envelope({
          spec: 'warrior-fury',
          gear: { head: 12640, main_hand: 11726 },
          talents: [2001, 2001, 2001, 2001, 2001, 2002, 2002],
          // IDS.md ids, not spell ids: the API does the mapping (amended contract).
          buffs: ['battle_shout', 'blessing_of_kings'],
          // Optional on SimInput and absent from the contract's row today. The stub sends
          // it so the happy path is testable; the absent case has its own test in Task 7,
          // and fromStoredCharacter refuses rather than substituting a race.
          race: 'orc',
          captured_at: '2026-09-14T09:40:00Z',
          source: 'addon',
        }),
    },
  ];

  async function handle(input: RequestInfo | URL, init?: RequestInit): Promise<Response> {
    const request = input instanceof Request ? input : new Request(input, init);
    lastUrl = request.url;
    lastHeaders = new Headers(request.headers);
    lastBody = null;
    if (request.method !== 'GET') {
      try {
        lastBody = await request.clone().json();
      } catch {
        lastBody = null;
      }
    }
    const path = new URL(request.url).pathname + new URL(request.url).search;
    for (const route of [...extra, ...builtIn]) {
      if (route.method !== request.method) continue;
      const match = route.pattern.exec(path);
      if (match !== null) return route.respond(match, request);
    }
    throw new Error(`unhandled request: ${request.method} ${request.url}`);
  }

  return {
    install() {
      vi.stubGlobal('fetch', vi.fn(handle));
    },
    reset() {
      vi.unstubAllGlobals();
      premium = true;
      lastUrl = '';
      lastBody = null;
      lastHeaders = null;
      extra = [];
      document.cookie = 'fs_csrf=; Max-Age=0; path=/';
    },
    setPremium(value) {
      premium = value;
    },
    route(route) {
      extra = [route, ...extra];
    },
    lastUrl: () => lastUrl,
    lastBody: () => lastBody,
    lastHeaders: () => lastHeaders,
  };
}
```

Two details that are easy to get wrong and will cost an hour each:

- the `/v1/sims/<id>` pattern must be tried **before** `/v1/sims`, and `/v1/sims/<id>/progress` before both, which is why `builtIn` is an ordered array and not an object;
- `reset()` clears `document.cookie`'s `fs_csrf`, the way `src/lib/account/api.test.ts`'s `afterEach` already does, so a test that sets it cannot leak a CSRF token into the next one.

- [ ] **Step 10: Write the client**

Create `web/src/lib/sim/api.ts`:

```ts
// web/src/lib/sim/api.ts
// Every call this lane makes to our API. It composes requestEnvelope from the account module
// rather than re-implementing the envelope, the session cookie and the CSRF header: that
// function is the one place a browser request to our API is built, and a second copy here
// would be a second place for the CSRF header to go wrong.
//
// Two rules the contract sets and this file enforces:
//   * a saved result carries its whole request, character included: it is plain JSON and a
//     few hundred bytes, and a saved sim that could not say what it simmed would be useless;
//   * POST /v1/sims/run answers 402 when the account is not premium. The envelope's error
//     has no machine-readable code in the phase-3 TypeScript mirror, so premium is keyed off
//     the status, and the copy is ours rather than the API's. Whether to offer the control at
//     all is decided from `user.premium` on GET /v1/me, not from a failed call.
import { AccountError, requestEnvelope } from '../account/api';
import type { CharacterPath } from '../characters';
import { API_BASE_URL } from '../planner/config';
import { simCopy } from './copy';
import type { SimInput, SimListPage, SimProgress, SimRequest, SimResult, SpecFidelity } from './types';

export const PREMIUM_REQUIRED_STATUS = 402;

export class SimApiError extends Error {
  constructor(
    message: string,
    readonly status: number,
  ) {
    super(message);
    this.name = 'SimApiError';
  }
}

/** Every failure this module raises, with the copy the page shows, keyed off the status. */
function asSimError(error: unknown, fallback: string): SimApiError {
  const status = error instanceof AccountError ? error.status : 0;
  if (status === PREMIUM_REQUIRED_STATUS) return new SimApiError(simCopy.premiumRequired, status);
  if (status === 404) return new SimApiError(simCopy.notFound, status);
  return new SimApiError(fallback, status);
}

async function call<T>(
  path: string,
  apiBase: string,
  fallback: string,
  init: { method?: string; body?: unknown; credentials?: RequestCredentials } = {},
): Promise<T> {
  let data: T | null;
  try {
    ({ data } = await requestEnvelope<T>(path, apiBase, { ...init, failureMessage: fallback }));
  } catch (error) {
    throw asSimError(error, fallback);
  }
  if (data === null) throw new SimApiError(fallback, 0);
  return data;
}

/** Saves a browser-run result. The whole request travels; it is plain JSON throughout. */
export async function saveSim(result: SimResult, apiBase: string = API_BASE_URL): Promise<string> {
  const body: SimResult = { ...result, lane: 'browser' };
  delete body.sim_id;
  const data = await call<{ sim_id: string }>('/v1/sims', apiBase, simCopy.saveFailed, {
    method: 'POST',
    body,
  });
  return data.sim_id;
}

export function fetchSim(simId: string, apiBase: string = API_BASE_URL): Promise<SimResult> {
  return call<SimResult>(`/v1/sims/${simId}`, apiBase, simCopy.loadFailed, { credentials: 'omit' });
}

export function listMySims(page: number = 1, apiBase: string = API_BASE_URL): Promise<SimListPage> {
  return call<SimListPage>(`/v1/sims?mine=1&page=${page}`, apiBase, simCopy.loadFailed);
}

/** The premium lane. Throws SimApiError with status 402 when the account is not premium. */
export async function dispatchServerSim(
  request: SimRequest,
  apiBase: string = API_BASE_URL,
): Promise<string> {
  const data = await call<{ sim_id: string }>('/v1/sims/run', apiBase, simCopy.saveFailed, {
    method: 'POST',
    body: request,
  });
  return data.sim_id;
}

export function fetchSimProgress(simId: string, apiBase: string = API_BASE_URL): Promise<SimProgress> {
  return call<SimProgress>(`/v1/sims/${simId}/progress`, apiBase, simCopy.loadFailed, {
    credentials: 'omit',
  });
}

export async function fetchSpecs(apiBase: string = API_BASE_URL): Promise<SpecFidelity[]> {
  const data = await call<{ specs: SpecFidelity[] }>('/v1/specs', apiBase, simCopy.specsFailed, {
    credentials: 'omit',
  });
  return data.specs;
}

/**
 * The character model the API holds, from the newest source it has. The route is three path
 * segments -- `/v1/characters/{region}/{ruleset}/{name}/sim-input` -- exactly as the existing
 * character route is spelled, never one percent-encoded `{character_key}` segment. Each
 * segment is encoded on its own so a name with a character needing escaping still resolves.
 */
export function fetchSimInput(path: CharacterPath, apiBase: string = API_BASE_URL): Promise<SimInput> {
  const segments = [path.region, path.ruleset, path.slug].map(encodeURIComponent).join('/');
  return call<SimInput>(`/v1/characters/${segments}/sim-input`, apiBase, simCopy.characterFailed);
}
```

# Group A — the engine in the browser

Tasks 3, 4 and 5, in order. Independent of groups B, C and E: nothing here imports a component, a page or the character model. It depends only on Task 1 and Task 2.
## Task 3: The four JSON entrypoints, the fake engine, and the loader

`sim.wasm` does not exist yet, and when it does it will be **ours**: built in this repository from the `sim/` Go module, which imports the engine as a library so that `sim/request` and `sim/adapter` run inside it. The browser therefore hands the wasm a `SimRequest` as JSON and gets a finished `SimResult` back, summary already built by the same Go code the server lane runs. This task builds the interface both the real wasm and the checked-in fake satisfy.

**Files:**
- Create: `web/src/lib/sim/engine.ts`
- Create: `web/src/fixtures/sim/engine-fake.ts`
- Create: `web/src/test-support/fake-worker.ts`
- Test: `web/src/fixtures/sim/engine-fake.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `ENGINE_VERSION`, `engineAssetUrl` (Task 1); `SimRequest`, `SimResult`, `SimProgressUpdate` (Task 2); the checked-in `web/src/fixtures/sim/result.json` (Task 2).
- Produces:
  - `web/src/lib/sim/engine.ts`: `type EngineMode = 'fake' | 'wasm'`, `const ENGINE_MODE: EngineMode`, `type ProgressHandler = (callbackId: string, progressJSON: string) => void`, `interface EngineModule { simRun(requestJSON: string, callbackId: string): Promise<string>; simSplit(requestJSON: string, n: number): string[]; simCombine(resultsJSON: string[]): string; simAbort(callbackId: string): void; onProgress(handler: ProgressHandler): void }`, `function loadEngine(version?: string): Promise<EngineModule>`.
  - `web/src/fixtures/sim/engine-fake.ts`: `interface FakeEngineOptions { tickMs?: number; ticks?: number; failWith?: string }`, `function createFakeEngine(options?: FakeEngineOptions): EngineModule`.
  - `web/src/test-support/fake-worker.ts`: `function createFakeWorker(engine: EngineModule): PoolWorker`, the in-process worker that speaks Task 4's message protocol over any `EngineModule`. **One copy, here, consumed by Tasks 5, 11 and 20** — each of those drives the pool end to end and an independent 40-line copy in three test files is three things to keep in step with one protocol.
  - Task 4 gives each worker one `EngineModule`; Task 5 never touches one directly.

### What the web does and does not do, now that no protobuf crosses a lane boundary

The contract is explicit: **"No protobuf crosses a lane boundary."** So this lane has no protobuf toolchain, no generated TypeScript bindings, no request encoder, and no TypeScript copy of the adapter's mapping table. There is no `raw.ts` and no `summarize.ts`. What the web does is `JSON.stringify(simRequest)` and `JSON.parse(simResult)`, and that is the whole of it.

The wasm exports exactly four functions, all JSON strings in and out. **The engine's own thirteen `js.Global().Set` entrypoints are an implementation detail behind these four and must not be called**, which the `EngineModule` interface enforces by not naming them:

| Function | In | Out |
|---|---|---|
| `simRun(requestJSON, callbackId)` | a `SimRequest` as JSON, and the id progress is reported under | a `SimResult` as JSON, summary included |
| `simSplit(requestJSON, n)` | the same request and a shard count | `n` request JSONs whose iterations sum to the original |
| `simCombine(resultsJSON)` | partial `SimResult` JSONs | one combined `SimResult` JSON |
| `simAbort(callbackId)` | the id of a run in flight | — |

`onProgress` is the fifth member of the interface and is **not** an engine entrypoint: it is the glue's registration hook. Go's `syscall/js` reports progress by calling a global the host installs, so the real loader sets `globalThis.simProgress` and the fake calls a stored handler. Keeping it on the interface is what lets `sim.worker.ts` be written once against both.

### The progress payload

The contract pins it: `Pick<SimResult, 'iterations_run' | 'dps'>`, which Task 2 mirrors as `SimProgressUpdate`. It is no new shape — the two fields of one that already exists — and they are exactly what the pool needs to pool an estimate: the iteration count, and the mean, standard deviation, min and max per shard. Engine Task 13 builds `sim/cmd/wasm` to it. **This is settled; nothing here asks the engine lane to confirm it**, and widening it would take a contract amendment, not a code change.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test for the fake engine**

Create `web/src/fixtures/sim/engine-fake.test.ts`:

```ts
import { describe, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest';
import type { SimProgressUpdate, SimRequest, SimResult } from '../../lib/sim/types';
import { DEFAULT_ENCOUNTER } from '../../lib/sim/types';
import { ENGINE_VERSION } from '../../lib/sim/version';
import { createFakeEngine } from './engine-fake';

const request: SimRequest = {
  engine_version: ENGINE_VERSION,
  spec: 'warrior-fury',
  source: { kind: 'addon', ref: '', captured_at: '2026-09-14T10:00:00Z' },
  character: {
    name: 'Thrallgar',
    race: 'orc',
    class: 'warrior',
    level: 60,
    talents: '-5530515-',
    gear: [{ slot: 'head', item_id: 12640 }],
    buffs: [],
    consumes: [],
  },
  encounter: DEFAULT_ENCOUNTER,
  iterations: 3000,
  random_seed: 7,
};

const json = (value: SimRequest): string => JSON.stringify(value);
const iterationsOf = (requestJSON: string): number => (JSON.parse(requestJSON) as SimRequest).iterations;
const resultOf = (resultJSON: string): SimResult => JSON.parse(resultJSON) as SimResult;

describe('simSplit', () => {
  it('splits iterations so the shards sum to the original', () => {
    const engine = createFakeEngine({ tickMs: 0 });
    const shards = engine.simSplit(json(request), 8);
    expect(shards).toHaveLength(8);
    expect(shards.reduce((sum, shard) => sum + iterationsOf(shard), 0)).toBe(3000);
  });

  it('never emits a shard with no iterations', () => {
    const engine = createFakeEngine({ tickMs: 0 });
    expect(engine.simSplit(json({ ...request, iterations: 3 }), 8)).toHaveLength(3);
  });

  it('returns requests, not bytes, so the pool can pass them straight back in', () => {
    const engine = createFakeEngine({ tickMs: 0 });
    const [first] = engine.simSplit(json(request), 4);
    expect(() => JSON.parse(first) as SimRequest).not.toThrow();
    expect((JSON.parse(first) as SimRequest).character.talents).toBe('-5530515-');
  });
});

describe('simRun', () => {
  it('reports progress before it resolves, and the progress sums to the shard', async () => {
    const engine = createFakeEngine({ tickMs: 0, ticks: 4 });
    const seen: number[] = [];
    engine.onProgress((callbackId, progressJSON) => {
      expect(callbackId).toBe('run-1');
      seen.push((JSON.parse(progressJSON) as SimProgressUpdate).iterations_run);
    });
    const result = resultOf(await engine.simRun(json({ ...request, iterations: 400 }), 'run-1'));
    expect(seen).toEqual([100, 200, 300, 400]);
    expect(result.iterations_run).toBe(400);
  });

  it('returns a finished SimResult, summary included, because the adapter runs inside', async () => {
    const engine = createFakeEngine({ tickMs: 0 });
    const result = resultOf(await engine.simRun(json(request), 'run-2'));
    expect(result.summary.engine_version).toBe(`sim:${ENGINE_VERSION}`);
    // The engine names abilities by action key, not display name (sim/adapter.ActionName);
    // resolving them is Task 23's job and no test here may assume a display name.
    expect(result.summary.damage_done[0].abilities[0].name).toMatch(/^(spell|item|other):/);
    expect(result.lane).toBe('browser');
  });

  it('gives the same numbers for the same seed, and different ones for a different seed', async () => {
    const engine = createFakeEngine({ tickMs: 0 });
    const a = resultOf(await engine.simRun(json(request), 'a'));
    const b = resultOf(await engine.simRun(json(request), 'b'));
    const c = resultOf(await engine.simRun(json({ ...request, random_seed: 8 }), 'c'));
    expect(a.dps.mean).toBe(b.dps.mean);
    expect(c.dps.mean).not.toBe(a.dps.mean);
  });

  it('rejects when the run is aborted mid-flight', async () => {
    vi.useFakeTimers();
    const engine = createFakeEngine({ tickMs: 10, ticks: 4 });
    const pending = engine.simRun(json(request), 'run-abort');
    const settled = expect(pending).rejects.toThrow('aborted');
    await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(15);
    engine.simAbort('run-abort');
    await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(50);
    await settled;
    vi.useRealTimers();
  });

  it('ignores an abort for a run that was never started', () => {
    const engine = createFakeEngine({ tickMs: 0 });
    expect(() => engine.simAbort('nobody')).not.toThrow();
  });
});

describe('simCombine', () => {
  it('pools the shard distributions into one finished result', async () => {
    const engine = createFakeEngine({ tickMs: 0 });
    const shards = engine.simSplit(json(request), 4);
    const results = await Promise.all(shards.map((shard, i) => engine.simRun(shard, `s${i}`)));
    const combined = resultOf(engine.simCombine(results));
    expect(combined.iterations_run).toBe(3000);
    expect(combined.dps.mean).toBeGreaterThan(0);
    expect(combined.dps.error).toBeCloseTo(combined.dps.stddev / Math.sqrt(3000), 9);
    expect(combined.summary.damage_done[0].guid).toBe('sim-player');
  });
});
```
