## Task 15: The unsupported-spec state and `/sim/specs`

Design 4.4, and the 4.1 state that reads "instead of a number, the spec's card from the support page". One card component serves both, which is why they are one task.

**Files:**
- Create: `web/src/lib/sim/spec-state.ts`
- Create: `web/src/components/sim/SpecCard.svelte`, `web/src/components/sim/SpecGrid.svelte`
- Modify: `web/src/components/sim/SimView.svelte`, `web/src/lib/sim/copy.ts`
- Test: `web/src/lib/sim/spec-state.test.ts`, `web/tests/e2e/sim-specs.spec.ts`

`web/src/lib/sim/specs.ts` is the **data lane's generated module** and has no test of this lane's to extend; Task 1's `spec-label.test.ts` covers the list itself. `mergeSpecRows` and the state vocabulary are new code in a new file, so they get their own test file.

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `SpecFidelity`, `SpecState` (Task 2); `fetchSpecs`, `simCopy` (Task 2); `specLabel`, `classOfSpec`, `dpsSpecs` (Task 1); `resolveActionName`, `ActionNames` (Task 23); `classColorVar` and `formatPercent` from `web/src/lib/report/format.ts`. `formatPercent(row.median_gap * 100)` is the site's one percentage formatter — one decimal and the `%` — and every other caller pre-multiplies by 100 exactly as this does; the card must not hand-roll `toFixed(1)`.
- Produces:
  - `web/src/lib/sim/spec-state.ts`: `function specStateLabel(state: SpecState): string`, `function specStateNote(state: SpecState): string`, `function specPillClass(state: SpecState): string`, `function mergeSpecRows(rows: readonly SpecFidelity[]): SpecFidelity[]`.
  - `SpecCard.svelte`: `let { row, actionNames = null, compact = false }: { row: SpecFidelity; actionNames?: ActionNames | null; compact?: boolean } = $props();`
  - `SpecGrid.svelte`: `let { rows, error }: { rows: SpecFidelity[] | null; error: string | null } = $props();`
  - `web/src/lib/sim/copy.ts`: the spec vocabulary — `specValidated`, `specInProgress`, `specNotYet` and their three notes, `specsIntro`, `specsLoading`, `specMedianGap`, `specOver`, `specParse`, `specParses`, `specNoParses`, `specWorstActions`, `specCast`, `specSimmed`.

### The card, decided

`bg-card-top border border-line rounded-panel p-4 flex flex-col gap-3`, with `id={row.spec}` so `/sim/specs#warrior-fury` from the settings bar's rotation link scrolls to it.

- **Title**: the spec name at `text-[15px] font-semibold`, in the class colour.
- **State pill**, immediately after the title:

| `state` | Pill | Class |
|---|---|---|
| `validated` | `Validated` | `pill pill-site` |
| `in_progress` | `In progress` | `pill pill-sample` |
| `unsupported` | `Not yet` | `pill pill-sample` |

- **The figure**, one mono line: `Median gap 3.1% over 50 parses`, whose percentage is `formatPercent(row.median_gap * 100)` — the site's one percentage formatter, never a local `toFixed`. When `median_gap` is null: `No parses yet`. When `parses` is under 50 and the state is `validated`, the row still says the real count — the contract's threshold is the API's business, and the page reports what it is told.
- **A sentence** in `text-muted text-[13px]`, from `specStateNote(row)`:
  - validated: *"Within 5% of the top 50 parses. Numbers from this spec are trustworthy."*
  - in_progress: *"Being corrected against real parses. Treat the number as a direction, not a figure."*
  - unsupported: *"Not modelled yet. Specs arrive in the order people are actually playing them."*
- **The largest gaps**, up to three rows, mono, `Heroic Strike 44 cast, 57 simmed` — the actual first, because the player's own number is the one they recognise. Omitted entirely when `worst_actions` is empty, rather than rendering an empty heading.
  The API's `worst_actions[].name` comes from `api sim-validate`, which compares a native engine result against a fight's summary, so it may be an engine action key (`spell:25286`) rather than a display name. Run it through `resolveActionName(action.name, actionNames)` (Task 23): a key passes through the resolver unchanged if it is already a name, so one call is correct whichever the API sends. `SpecCard` therefore takes an `actionNames: ActionNames | null` prop — on `/sim/specs`, where cards for every class are shown at once, that prop is `null` and the keys render as keys; the page is about fidelity figures, not ability names, and loading nine class tables for three rows of text is not worth the bytes. In the **in-page** unsupported state the store already has the character's class table loaded, so it is passed.
- **Footer**: `engineLabel(row.engine_version)` then the date — "Engine df3b36e4c · 2026-09-14" — mono, muted. When `engine_version` is null: just the date.

`compact` drops the gap rows and the footer, for the in-page unsupported state where the card is one item in a column rather than one of many in a grid.

### The page, decided

`/sim/specs` renders: the heading row (from `SimView`), one paragraph of standing copy, then `SpecGrid` — `grid grid-cols-1 gap-3 md:grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-3`, one card per row of `dpsSpecs()` (Task 1), in the generated list's own order, which is class slug then tree position. A spec in the list with no row from the API renders as `unsupported` with zero parses, so the grid is always `dpsSpecs().length` cards and nobody has to wonder whether a spec is missing or unlisted. **Tanks and healers are not on this page**: launch scope is Quick Sim for damage specs, and a `Protection Warrior` card reading `Not yet` would promise something the design has explicitly deferred.

`specsIntro`: *"The simulator is only worth as much as its numbers. A nightly job sims the top 50 parses for each spec and publishes the gap here, whatever it is."*

While loading: the grid renders `dpsSpecs().length` skeleton cards at the same height, so the page does not reflow when the answer lands — the LCP budget is measured on this page too. On failure: one `role="alert"` line with `simCopy.specsFailed` and a `Try again` button.

### The in-page unsupported state

When `store.character !== null` and that spec's row is `unsupported`, `SimView` renders — **instead of** the run control, the sentence and the results, not above them — the settings bar (still useful: it says what would be simulated) and one `SpecCard` with `compact`, preceded by one line: `simCopy.specUnsupportedLead`, *"The simulator does not model this spec yet."*

describe('specStateLabel and specStateNote', () => {
  it('names each state the way the support page shows it', () => {
    expect(specStateLabel('validated')).toBe(simCopy.specValidated);
    expect(specStateLabel('in_progress')).toBe(simCopy.specInProgress);
    expect(specStateLabel('unsupported')).toBe(simCopy.specNotYet);

    expect(simCopy.specValidated).toBe('Validated');
    expect(simCopy.specInProgress).toBe('In progress');

    expect(specStateNote('validated')).toContain('trustworthy');
    expect(specStateNote('in_progress')).toContain('a direction, not a figure');
    expect(specStateNote('unsupported')).toContain('the order people are actually playing them');

describe('mergeSpecRows', () => {
  it('returns one row per spec in the canonical list, in its order', () => {
    const merged = mergeSpecRows([
      {
        spec: 'mage-frost',
        state: 'validated',
        median_gap: 0.02,
        parses: 50,
        worst_actions: [],
        engine_version: ENGINE_VERSION,
        updated_at: '2026-09-14T04:12:00Z',

    expect(merged).toHaveLength(dpsSpecs().length);

        parses: 50,

        engine_version: ENGINE_VERSION,
        updated_at: '2026-09-14T04:12:00Z',

- [ ] **Step 2: Add `mergeSpecRows` and the state vocabulary**

These belong beside the spec list rather than in the generated file, so create `web/src/lib/sim/spec-state.ts`:

```ts
// web/src/lib/sim/spec-state.ts
// How a spec's fidelity reads, and the rule that the support page always shows every spec.
//
// The grid is always the canonical list, never the API's answer: one card per dps spec, in
// rankings-population order, so a spec with no row reads "Not yet" rather than being absent.
// An absent card is indistinguishable from a page that failed to load half its content, and
// "can I trust this" is the question this page exists to answer.
import { simCopy } from './copy';
import { dpsSpecs } from './spec-label';
import type { SpecFidelity, SpecState } from './types';

// The six sentences live in copy.ts with every other user-visible string on this lane; the
// mapping from state to sentence lives here, because it is logic and not copy.
const LABELS: Record<SpecState, string> = {
  validated: simCopy.specValidated,
  in_progress: simCopy.specInProgress,
  unsupported: simCopy.specNotYet,
};

const NOTES: Record<SpecState, string> = {
  validated: simCopy.specValidatedNote,
  in_progress: simCopy.specInProgressNote,
  unsupported: simCopy.specNotYetNote,
};

export function specStateLabel(state: SpecState): string {
  return LABELS[state];
}

export function specStateNote(state: SpecState): string {
  return NOTES[state];
}

export function specPillClass(state: SpecState): string {
  return state === 'validated' ? 'pill pill-site' : 'pill pill-sample';
}

export function mergeSpecRows(rows: readonly SpecFidelity[]): SpecFidelity[] {
  const bySpec = new Map(rows.map((row) => [row.spec, row]));
  return dpsSpecs().map(
    (entry) =>
      bySpec.get(entry.spec) ?? {
        spec: entry.spec,
        state: 'unsupported' as const,
        median_gap: null,
        parses: 0,
        worst_actions: [],
        engine_version: null,
        updated_at: '',
      },
  );
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `SpecCard.svelte`**

```svelte
<!-- web/src/components/sim/SpecCard.svelte -->
<!-- "Can I trust this" in one card. The figure is whatever the validation job measured,
     published either way -- the design's whole point is that the answer lives on the site
     rather than in a footnote, so a bad gap is shown as plainly as a good one.
     The cast-gap rows read actual first: the player's own number is the one they recognise
     from their own log, and the simmed one is the claim being made about it. -->
<script lang="ts">
  // formatPercent is the site's one percentage formatter: one decimal and a % sign. Every
  // other caller in report/format.ts pre-multiplies by 100 exactly as this does, so a
  // hand-rolled toFixed(1) here would be a second formatter that drifts the day the first
  // one changes.
  import { classColorVar, formatPercent } from '../../lib/report/format';
  import { resolveActionName, type ActionNames } from '../../lib/sim/action-names';
  import { simCopy } from '../../lib/sim/copy';
  import { specPillClass, specStateLabel, specStateNote } from '../../lib/sim/spec-state';
  import { classOfSpec, specLabel } from '../../lib/sim/spec-label';
  import type { SpecFidelity } from '../../lib/sim/types';

  let {
    row,
    actionNames = null,
    compact = false,
  }: {
    row: SpecFidelity;
    /**
     * The build's name table (Task 23), for `worst_actions[].name`. `api sim-validate`
     * compares a native engine result against a fight's summary, so a name there may be an
     * engine action key. Null on `/sim/specs`, where cards for nine classes are shown at
     * once and loading nine tables for three rows of text is not worth the bytes; the
     * in-page unsupported card passes the store's, which is already loaded.
     */
    actionNames?: ActionNames | null;
    compact?: boolean;
  } = $props();

  const colour = $derived(classColorVar(classOfSpec(row.spec)));
  const figure = $derived(
    row.median_gap === null
      ? simCopy.specNoParses
      : `${simCopy.specMedianGap} ${formatPercent(row.median_gap * 100)} ${simCopy.specOver} ${row.parses} ${row.parses === 1 ? simCopy.specParse : simCopy.specParses}`,
  );
  const footer = $derived(
    [row.engine_version === null ? '' : `Engine ${row.engine_version}`, row.updated_at.slice(0, 10)]
      .filter((part) => part !== '')
      .join(' · '),
  );
</script>

<article
  id={row.spec}
  class="border-line bg-card-top rounded-panel flex flex-col gap-3 border p-4"
  data-testid={`spec-${row.spec}`}
>
  <div class="flex flex-wrap items-center gap-2">
    <h3 class="text-[15px] font-semibold" style={`color: ${colour}`}>{specLabel(row.spec)}</h3>
    <span class={specPillClass(row.state)} data-testid="spec-state">{specStateLabel(row.state)}</span>
  </div>

  <p class="tabular text-strong font-mono text-[13px]" data-testid="spec-figure">{figure}</p>
  <p class="text-muted text-[13px]">{specStateNote(row.state)}</p>

  {#if !compact && row.worst_actions.length > 0}
    <div class="flex flex-col gap-1">
      <span class="label text-muted">{simCopy.specWorstActions}</span>
      {#each row.worst_actions.slice(0, 3) as action (action.name)}
        <span class="tabular text-muted font-mono text-[12px]">
          {resolveActionName(action.name, actionNames)}
          {action.actual_casts}
          {simCopy.specCast}, {action.sim_casts}
          {simCopy.specSimmed}
        </span>
      {/each}
    </div>
  {/if}

  {#if !compact && footer !== ''}
    <p class="tabular text-muted mt-auto font-mono text-[12px]" data-testid="spec-footer">{footer}</p>
  {/if}
</article>
```

  specInProgress: 'In progress',
  specNotYet: 'Not yet',

  specInProgressNote:
    'Being corrected against real parses. Treat the number as a direction, not a figure.',
  specNotYetNote:
    'Not modelled yet. Specs arrive in the order people are actually playing them.',
  specMedianGap: 'Median gap',
  specOver: 'over',

  specParses: 'parses',

- [ ] **Step 4: Write `SpecGrid.svelte`**

Three states in one component: `rows === null && error === null` renders `dpsSpecs().length` skeleton articles at `min-h-[168px]` with `bg-card-top border border-line rounded-panel` and nothing inside, so the grid's height is the same before and after the fetch and the page does not reflow. `error !== null` renders one `role="alert"` paragraph and a `Try again` button that raises `onretry`. Otherwise it renders `mergeSpecRows(rows)` as `SpecCard`s in a `grid grid-cols-1 gap-3 md:grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-3`.

- [ ] **Step 5: Wire both into `SimView.svelte`**

`SimView` gains a `view` prop read from `data-sim-view` (`'sim' | 'specs'`). When it is `'specs'`, the whole body is `specsIntro` plus `SpecGrid`, fetched once in a `$effect` through `fetchSpecs`. When it is `'sim'` and the loaded character's spec merges to `unsupported`, the run control, sentence and results are replaced by `specUnsupportedLead` and a compact `SpecCard`. `SimView` fetches the spec list on mount in both cases; it is one small, cacheable, credential-free GET and the page needs it either way.

## Task 16: Compare mode

Design 4.2: the character loaded from a fight's recorded gear, the sim run, and two columns — simulated and actual — per ability and per buff, with the differences explained in words.

**Files:**
- Create: `web/src/lib/sim/compare.ts`, `web/src/components/sim/CompareView.svelte`
- Modify: `web/src/components/sim/SimView.svelte`, `web/src/components/report/ReportView.svelte`, `web/src/lib/sim/copy.ts`
- Modify: `web/src/lib/sim/store.svelte.ts` (Step 5 adds `setMessage`)
- Test: `web/src/lib/sim/compare.test.ts`, `web/tests/e2e/sim-compare.spec.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Summary` from `web/src/lib/report/types.ts`; `fetchReportMeta`, `fetchSummary` from `web/src/lib/report/load.ts`; `playerActor` (Task 14); `resolveActionName`, `SYNTHETIC_ID_BASE`, `ActionNames` (Task 23); `parseFightRef` (Task 7, in `SimView`'s compare entry) and `store.loadFight`, which is `fromLoggedFight` behind the store; `simCopy` (Task 2). **Not** `namedSummary` (the join needs raw ids), **not** `formatPercent` (this view's shares are whole percentages of two different durations, computed in `compareSummaries`) and **not** `formatDuration` (the two durations are printed by `CompareView`'s own footnote line, which says the lengths differ rather than printing them).
- Produces:
  - `web/src/lib/sim/compare.ts`: `interface AbilityDiff`, `interface AuraDiff`, `interface Comparison`, `const MAX_ABILITY_LINES`, `const MAX_AURA_LINES`, `function compareSummaries(sim: Summary, actual: Summary, actualPlayerName: string, names: ActionNames | null): Comparison`. It takes the **raw** sim summary and the name table, not a `namedSummary()` copy: it needs the raw `spell_id` for the id tier of the join and the resolved name for the name tier. Its one sentence is `simCopy.compareNoPlayer`.
  - `CompareView.svelte`: `let { comparison, simDuration, actualDuration }: { comparison: Comparison; simDuration: number; actualDuration: number } = $props();`, loaded through `createLazyComponent(() => import('./CompareView.svelte'))` for the same budget reason `SimResults` is.
  - `web/src/lib/sim/store.svelte.ts` gains `setMessage(text: string)` (Step 5).

### The two sides identify a row differently, and that is the whole difficulty

This is the one place a sim summary and a logged summary meet, and they do not agree. The fight's rows carry the combat log's client spell ids and its own display names (`23894`, `Bloodthirst`); the sim's carry the adapter's row ids and action keys, which agree with the client for a plain untagged spell (`23894`, `spell:23894`) and deliberately do not for anything else (`20022000007`, `other:attack/2`).

So the join is two-tiered, in this order, and `compareSummaries` does it on the raw summaries, not on resolved names:

1. **`spell_id` below `SYNTHETIC_ID_BASE` (2,000,000)** — that is a real client id on both sides, and it is the right key for every ordinary ability. It is also the only key that survives a build in which the site has no name table loaded.
2. **The resolved display name, trimmed and case-folded**, for a sim row whose id is at or above the reserved base. Those are the tagged, ranked, item and `other` rows, whose ids are the adapter's own arithmetic and mean nothing to a combat log. `resolveActionName('other:attack', names)` is `Attack`, which `simCopy.proseNames` is not applied to here — a table says `Attack`, a sentence says "white hits".

Anything still unmatched is its own row with a blank on one side. That is the honest outcome: the table then says the sim cast something the fight did not, which is visible, rather than silently folding two different actions together.

**Never join on `spell_id` alone.** Every `other:` row the sim emits has a derived id in the reserved space and a logged melee row has spell id 0 or 1; matching those by number would pair a white swing with whatever the log happened to record at that id. **And never key a rendered row on the name** — the `{#each}` key is the sim row's `spell_id`, which `sim/adapter` guarantees unique (`ErrDuplicateRow`), because a repeated key is a Svelte 5 runtime error.

`SYNTHETIC_ID_BASE` is exported from `web/src/lib/sim/action-names.ts` (`export const SYNTHETIC_ID_BASE = 2_000_000;`) with a comment naming `sim/adapter/identity.go`'s `syntheticBase` as its source, and `compare.test.ts` covers all three cases: a plain spell matched by id, a tagged action matched by resolved name, and an action matched by neither.

### No second parse, and no second API

The contract is explicit: "Compare mode reads the fight summary from the report's existing `data_base_url`; no new API." So compare mode fetches exactly two things — `GET /v1/reports/{id}` for the base url, and `${base}/fights/{n}/summary.json` — through the report lane's own `fetchReportMeta` and `fetchSummary`. It never parses, never re-summarises, and never asks the API for a comparison.

### The layout, decided

Compare mode **replaces** the sentence and the results, and keeps the strip, the settings bar and the run control. `?mode=compare&ref=<report>:<fight>` is what opens it.

1. **The headline**, one `text-[15px] text-strong` line: *"This fight did 1,175 DPS; the sim expects 1,400. That is 84% of what this gear can do."* The two numbers are the two runs' own roster DPS, and the percentage is actual over simmed, rounded. `compare.test.ts` builds both summaries itself with round figures rather than reading the fixture, so the arithmetic in the assertion is checkable by eye. It is the answer; everything below it is the working.
2. **The lines**, a `<ul>` of at most eight `text-[14px]` sentences, five cast gaps then three uptime gaps, largest first, each exactly as the design writes them: *"Bloodthirst cast 33 times, the sim expects 41."* and *"Flurry uptime 61% against 78%."* This is what the design means by "explained in words", and it comes before the tables because most players will read it and stop.
3. **Two column headings** — `This fight` then `Simulated`, in that order, because the fight is the thing the player recognises.
4. **The ability table**: one row per ability either run had. `grid-cols-[minmax(0,1fr)_auto_auto]` on phone, `grid-cols-[minmax(0,1.6fr)_72px_72px_88px_88px]` from md: name, actual casts, sim casts, actual damage, sim damage. Rows ordered by the larger of the two damage figures. The two casts columns sit side by side so the eye reads the pair, not the rows.
5. **The buff table**: name, actual uptime, sim uptime, each as a whole percentage of that run's own duration — never of the other's, which is why `compareSummaries` divides each by its own `duration_ms`.
6. **A footnote**: `simCopy.compareFootnote`, *"The two fights are not the same length; every share is of its own fight."*

Differences are not coloured. A red-and-green diff would be the fourth colour vocabulary on the page and the design system forbids colour-only signalling; the words above the table already say which way each gap runs.

Reached from three places, all writing the same URL: the report's fight header gets a `Sim this fight` link (this task adds it to `ReportView.svelte`), a ranking row's execution cell already links there (Task 10), and the sim page's own fight source card.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `web/src/lib/sim/compare.test.ts`. It builds **both** summaries itself, with round numbers, rather than reading `fixtureResult`: the point of this test is the join and the arithmetic, and a reader has to be able to check 1,200 over 1,500 without opening a fixture. The shapes are the real ones — the sim side carries engine action keys and derived row ids, the fight side carries the combat log's client spell ids and display names — because that difference is the whole thing under test.

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import type { ActionNames } from './action-names';
import type { Summary } from '../report/types';
import { simCopy } from './copy';
import { compareSummaries } from './compare';

const names: ActionNames = {
  spell: { '25286': 'Heroic Strike', '23894': 'Bloodthirst', '12974': 'Flurry', '12292': 'Death Wish' },
  item: {},
};

const empty = {
  damage_taken: [],
  healing: [],
  healing_taken: [],
  deaths: [],
  interrupts: [],
  dispels: [],
  resources: [],
  threat: [],
  combatants: [],
  roster: [],
};

function ability(spellId: number, name: string, total: number) {
  return { spell_id: spellId, name, total, effective: total, hits: 1, crits: 0, ticks: 0, min: 1, max: 1 };
}

/**
 * The sim: 270,000 over 180 s is 1,500 DPS. Its melee is two tagged rows at derived ids,
 * exactly as sim/adapter emits them; everything else is a plain spell at its client id.
 */
function simSummary(): Summary {
  return {
    ...empty,
    engine_version: 'sim:test',
    fight_index: 1,
    duration_ms: 180_000,
    damage_done: [
      {
        guid: 'sim-player',
        name: 'Fury',
        class: 'warrior',
        total: 270_000,
        effective: 270_000,
        active_ms: 180_000,
        targets: [],
        series: [],
        abilities: [
          ability(25286, 'spell:25286', 120_000),
          ability(10022000007, 'other:attack/1', 60_000),
          ability(20022000007, 'other:attack/2', 30_000),
          ability(23894, 'spell:23894', 60_000),
        ],
      },
    ],
    casts: [
      castRow('sim-player', 25286, 'spell:25286', 41),
      castRow('sim-player', 23894, 'spell:23894', 22),
    ],
    auras: [
      auraRow('sim-player', 12974, 'spell:12974', 140_400),
      auraRow('sim-player', 12292, 'spell:12292', 108_000),
    ],
  };
}

/** The same fight, played: 216,000 over 180 s is 1,200 DPS, which is 80% of the sim. */
function actualSummary(): Summary {
  return {
    ...empty,
    engine_version: '0.5.3',
    fight_index: 1,
    duration_ms: 180_000,
    damage_done: [
      {
        guid: 'log-player',
        name: 'Thrallgar',
        class: 'Warrior',
        total: 216_000,
        effective: 216_000,
        active_ms: 180_000,
        targets: [],
        series: [],
        abilities: [
          ability(25286, 'Heroic Strike', 96_000),
          // A combat log records one melee row and gives it spell id 0.
          ability(0, 'Melee', 72_000),
          ability(23894, 'Bloodthirst', 48_000),
        ],
      },
    ],
    casts: [
      castRow('log-player', 25286, 'Heroic Strike', 33),
      castRow('log-player', 23894, 'Bloodthirst', 19),
    ],
    auras: [
      auraRow('log-player', 12974, 'Flurry', 109_800),
      auraRow('log-player', 12292, 'Death Wish', 72_000),
    ],
  };
}

function castRow(guid: string, spellId: number, spellName: string, succeeded: number) {
  return {
    guid,
    name: guid,
    spell_id: spellId,
    spell_name: spellName,
    started: succeeded,
    succeeded,
    failed: 0,
    cast_time_ms: 0,
    sequence: [],
  };
}

function auraRow(guid: string, spellId: number, name: string, uptimeMs: number) {
  return {
    target_guid: guid,
    target_name: guid,
    spell_id: spellId,
    name,
    type: 'BUFF',
    applications: 1,
    max_stacks: 1,
    uptime_ms: uptimeMs,
    segments: [],
    appliers: [guid],
  };
}

describe('compareSummaries', () => {
  it('leads with the two DPS figures and what the fraction means', () => {
    const comparison = compareSummaries(simSummary(), actualSummary(), 'Thrallgar', names);
    expect(comparison.headline).toBe(
      'This fight did 1,200 DPS; the sim expects 1,500. That is 80% of what this gear can do.',
    );
    expect(comparison.executionScore).toBeCloseTo(0.8, 6);
  });

  it('joins a plain spell by its client id, whatever the two sides call it', () => {
    // The sim row is named `spell:25286`, the fight row `Heroic Strike`, and both carry
    // spell id 25286 -- so they are one row, and the name shown is the resolved one.
    const comparison = compareSummaries(simSummary(), actualSummary(), 'Thrallgar', names);
    const row = comparison.abilities.find((entry) => entry.name === 'Heroic Strike');
    expect(row).toEqual({
      // The row's identity is the client spell id, which is also its test id in CompareView.
      spellId: 25286,
      name: 'Heroic Strike',
      simCasts: 41,
      actualCasts: 33,
      simDamage: 120_000,
      actualDamage: 96_000,
    });
  });

  it('joins the sim’s tagged melee rows onto the log’s single one, by name', () => {
    // Every derived id is at or above SYNTHETIC_ID_BASE and means nothing to a combat log,
    // and the log's own melee row carries spell id 0 -- so neither side can be joined by
    // number here. The two tagged sim rows fold onto one, and their damage sums to 90,000.
    const comparison = compareSummaries(simSummary(), actualSummary(), 'Thrallgar', names);
    const row = comparison.abilities.find((entry) => entry.name === 'Melee');
    expect(row?.simDamage).toBe(90_000);
    expect(row?.actualDamage).toBe(72_000);
    // A name-keyed row still gets an integer identity: the sim side's first row id.
    expect(row?.spellId).toBe(10022000007);
  });

  it('never joins two different actions by number just because both are id zero', () => {
    const sim = simSummary();
    const actual = actualSummary();
    // A second log row at spell id 0 -- a environmental hit, say -- must stay its own row.
    actual.damage_done[0].abilities.push(ability(0, 'Falling', 1));
    actual.damage_done[0].total += 1;
    const comparison = compareSummaries(sim, actual, 'Thrallgar', names);
    expect(comparison.abilities.map((row) => row.name)).toContain('Falling');
    expect(comparison.abilities.find((row) => row.name === 'Falling')?.simDamage).toBe(0);
    // Every row's identity is distinct, which is what CompareView's {#each} key needs.
    const ids = comparison.abilities.map((row) => row.spellId);
    expect(new Set(ids).size).toBe(ids.length);
  });

  it('pairs every ability the two runs have between them, biggest first', () => {
    const comparison = compareSummaries(simSummary(), actualSummary(), 'Thrallgar', names);
    expect(comparison.abilities.map((row) => row.name)).toEqual([
      'Heroic Strike',
      'Melee',
      'Bloodthirst',
    ]);
  });

  it('explains each cast-count gap in words, biggest first', () => {
    const comparison = compareSummaries(simSummary(), actualSummary(), 'Thrallgar', names);
    expect(comparison.lines[0]).toBe('Heroic Strike cast 33 times, the sim expects 41.');
    expect(comparison.lines).toContain('Bloodthirst cast 19 times, the sim expects 22.');
  });

  it('explains each uptime gap the way the design writes it', () => {
    const comparison = compareSummaries(simSummary(), actualSummary(), 'Thrallgar', names);
    expect(comparison.lines).toContain('Flurry uptime 61% against 78%.');
    expect(comparison.lines).toContain('Death Wish uptime 40% against 60%.');
  });

  it('falls back to the action key rather than a blank when the build has no name table', () => {
    const comparison = compareSummaries(simSummary(), actualSummary(), 'Thrallgar', null);
    expect(comparison.abilities.map((row) => row.name)).toContain('spell:25286');
  });

  it('clamps the score to the range the contract stores', () => {
    const actual = actualSummary();
    actual.damage_done[0].total *= 9;
    expect(compareSummaries(simSummary(), actual, 'Thrallgar', names).executionScore).toBe(2);
  });

  it('says so when the fight has no damage for this character', () => {
    const comparison = compareSummaries(simSummary(), actualSummary(), 'Someone Else', names);
    expect(comparison.headline).toBe(simCopy.compareNoPlayer);
    expect(comparison.executionScore).toBeNull();
    expect(comparison.lines).toEqual([]);
  });

  it('lists no more than five ability lines and three aura lines', () => {
    const comparison = compareSummaries(simSummary(), actualSummary(), 'Thrallgar', names);
    expect(comparison.lines.length).toBeLessThanOrEqual(MAX_ABILITY_LINES + MAX_AURA_LINES);
  });
});
```

`MAX_ABILITY_LINES` and `MAX_AURA_LINES` are imported from `./compare` alongside `compareSummaries`.

Expected: FAIL. Then create `web/src/lib/sim/compare.ts`:

```ts
// web/src/lib/sim/compare.ts
// A sim beside the logged fight it was built from, per ability and per buff.
//
// The one hard part is the join, and it is hard because the two sides identify a row
// differently. sim/adapter gives a summary row a unique integer: the client spell id for a
// plain untagged spell, and a derived id at or above SYNTHETIC_ID_BASE for a tagged or
// ranked spell, an item or an "other" action. A combat log carries the client's own ids and
// the client's own names, and gives every melee swing spell id 0.
//
// So the key is two-tiered, in this order:
//
//   1. `id:<n>` when the row's id is a real client id (below SYNTHETIC_ID_BASE and non-zero).
//      That is the right key for every ordinary ability and the only one that works with no
//      name table loaded.
//   2. `name:<resolved, de-variant, aliased, case-folded>` otherwise -- the tagged, item and
//      "other" rows, whose ids are the adapter's own arithmetic, plus the log's id-zero
//      rows. The variant suffix is dropped so the sim's tagged melee rows fold onto
//      one, and simCopy.actionAliases turns the engine's "Attack" into the log's "Melee".
//
// Anything matched by neither is its own row with a zero on one side. That is the honest
// outcome: the table then says the sim did something the fight did not, which is visible,
// rather than folding two different actions together, which is not. In particular two
// id-zero rows on the log side stay apart, because tier 2 keys them by name.
import { SYNTHETIC_ID_BASE, resolveActionName, type ActionNames } from './action-names';
import { simCopy } from './copy';
import type { Summary } from '../report/types';
import { playerActor } from './sentence';

export interface AbilityDiff {
  /**
   * The row's own identity, and what the `{#each}` key and the test id are built from --
   * never the name, which is display text and can contain a space, a colon or a slash.
   * It is the sim side's `spell_id` for the key when there is one, else the fight side's:
   * both are stable, and join keys are unique, so no two rows can collide.
   */
  spellId: number;
  name: string;
  simCasts: number;
  actualCasts: number;
  simDamage: number;
  actualDamage: number;
}

export interface AuraDiff {
  name: string;
  simUptimeMs: number;
  actualUptimeMs: number;
}

export interface Comparison {
  headline: string;
  simDps: number;
  actualDps: number;
  /** actual / simulated, clamped to [0, 2] as the contract clamps the stored column. */
  executionScore: number | null;
  abilities: AbilityDiff[];
  auras: AuraDiff[];
  /** The differences in words, largest gap first: at most five abilities and three auras. */
  lines: string[];
}

export const MAX_ABILITY_LINES = 5;
export const MAX_AURA_LINES = 3;

/** "Heroic Strike (2, Rank 3)" is the same action as "Heroic Strike" for a join. */
const VARIANT = /\s*\((?:\d+(?:, Rank \d+)?|Rank \d+)\)$/;

/** One row's join key, and the name it should be shown under. */
interface Keyed {
  key: string;
  label: string;
}

function keyed(spellId: number, rawName: string, names: ActionNames | null): Keyed {
  const label = resolveActionName(rawName, names);
  if (spellId > 0 && spellId < SYNTHETIC_ID_BASE) return { key: `id:${spellId}`, label };
  const base = label.replace(VARIANT, '');
  const aliased = simCopy.actionAliases[base] ?? base;
  return { key: `name:${aliased.trim().toLowerCase()}`, label: aliased };
}

function dpsOf(summary: Summary, total: number): number {
  return summary.duration_ms <= 0 ? 0 : (total / summary.duration_ms) * 1000;
}

/** Successful casts per join key, for one actor. */
function castsByKey(summary: Summary, guid: string, names: ActionNames | null): Map<string, number> {
  const casts = new Map<string, number>();
  for (const row of summary.casts) {
    if (row.guid !== guid) continue;
    const { key } = keyed(row.spell_id, row.spell_name, names);
    casts.set(key, (casts.get(key) ?? 0) + row.succeeded);
  }
  return casts;
}

/** Damage per join key, and the label each key should be shown under. */
function damageByKey(
  abilities: readonly { spell_id: number; name: string; total: number }[],
  names: ActionNames | null,
): { damage: Map<string, number>; labels: Map<string, string>; ids: Map<string, number> } {
  const damage = new Map<string, number>();
  const labels = new Map<string, string>();
  const ids = new Map<string, number>();
  for (const ability of abilities) {
    const { key, label } = keyed(ability.spell_id, ability.name, names);
    damage.set(key, (damage.get(key) ?? 0) + ability.total);
    // First one wins: the sim side is read first, so a resolved engine name beats a raw key.
    if (!labels.has(key)) labels.set(key, label);
    if (!ids.has(key)) ids.set(key, ability.spell_id);
  }
  return { damage, labels, ids };
}

/** Buff uptime per join key, for one actor. */
function upByKey(summary: Summary, guid: string, names: ActionNames | null): Map<string, number> {
  const up = new Map<string, number>();
  for (const track of summary.auras) {
    if (track.target_guid !== guid || track.type !== 'BUFF') continue;
    const { key } = keyed(track.spell_id, track.name, names);
    up.set(key, Math.max(up.get(key) ?? 0, track.uptime_ms));
  }
  return up;
}

function labelsOf(summary: Summary, guid: string, names: ActionNames | null): Map<string, string> {
  const labels = new Map<string, string>();
  for (const track of summary.auras) {
    if (track.target_guid !== guid || track.type !== 'BUFF') continue;
    const { key, label } = keyed(track.spell_id, track.name, names);
    if (!labels.has(key)) labels.set(key, label);
  }
  return labels;
}

function pct(part: number, whole: number): number {
  return whole <= 0 ? 0 : Math.round((part / whole) * 100);
}

function round(value: number): string {
  return Math.round(value).toLocaleString('en-US');
}

/**
 * `actualPlayerName` names the character in the logged fight; the sim has exactly one
 * player, so its actor is whichever did the most damage. `names` is the build's name table
 * (Task 23) and may be null, in which case a sim row keeps its action key and only the
 * id-keyed rows pair up -- which is still most of the table.
 *
 * `sim` is the raw summary, not a `namedSummary()` copy: this function resolves names
 * itself, because it needs the raw `spell_id` for tier 1 and the resolved name for tier 2.
 */
export function compareSummaries(
  sim: Summary,
  actual: Summary,
  actualPlayerName: string,
  names: ActionNames | null,
): Comparison {
  const simActor = playerActor(sim);
  const actualActor = actual.damage_done.find((row) => row.name === actualPlayerName) ?? null;

  if (simActor === null || actualActor === null) {
    return {
      headline: simCopy.compareNoPlayer,
      simDps: 0,
      actualDps: 0,
      executionScore: null,
      abilities: [],
      auras: [],
      lines: [],
    };
  }

  const simDps = dpsOf(sim, simActor.total);
  const actualDps = dpsOf(actual, actualActor.total);
  const executionScore = simDps <= 0 ? null : Math.min(2, Math.max(0, actualDps / simDps));

  const simCasts = castsByKey(sim, simActor.guid, names);
  const actualCasts = castsByKey(actual, actualActor.guid, names);
  const simSide = damageByKey(simActor.abilities, names);
  const actualSide = damageByKey(actualActor.abilities, names);

  const abilityKeys = [...new Set([...simSide.damage.keys(), ...actualSide.damage.keys()])];
  const abilities: AbilityDiff[] = abilityKeys
    .map((key) => ({
      spellId: simSide.ids.get(key) ?? actualSide.ids.get(key) ?? 0,
      name: simSide.labels.get(key) ?? actualSide.labels.get(key) ?? key,
      simCasts: simCasts.get(key) ?? 0,
      actualCasts: actualCasts.get(key) ?? 0,
      simDamage: simSide.damage.get(key) ?? 0,
      actualDamage: actualSide.damage.get(key) ?? 0,
    }))
    .sort((a, b) => Math.max(b.simDamage, b.actualDamage) - Math.max(a.simDamage, a.actualDamage));

  const simUp = upByKey(sim, simActor.guid, names);
  const actualUp = upByKey(actual, actualActor.guid, names);
  const simAuraLabels = labelsOf(sim, simActor.guid, names);
  const actualAuraLabels = labelsOf(actual, actualActor.guid, names);
  const auras: AuraDiff[] = [...new Set([...simUp.keys(), ...actualUp.keys()])]
    .map((key) => ({
      name: simAuraLabels.get(key) ?? actualAuraLabels.get(key) ?? key,
      simUptimeMs: simUp.get(key) ?? 0,
      actualUptimeMs: actualUp.get(key) ?? 0,
    }))
    .sort((a, b) => Math.max(b.simUptimeMs, b.actualUptimeMs) - Math.max(a.simUptimeMs, a.actualUptimeMs));

  const castLines = [...abilities]
    .filter((row) => row.simCasts !== row.actualCasts)
    .sort((a, b) => Math.abs(b.simCasts - b.actualCasts) - Math.abs(a.simCasts - a.actualCasts))
    .slice(0, MAX_ABILITY_LINES)
    .map((row) => `${row.name} cast ${row.actualCasts} times, the sim expects ${row.simCasts}.`);

  const share = (ms: number, summary: Summary): number => ms / Math.max(summary.duration_ms, 1);
  const auraLines = [...auras]
    .filter((row) => pct(row.simUptimeMs, sim.duration_ms) !== pct(row.actualUptimeMs, actual.duration_ms))
    .sort(
      (a, b) =>
        Math.abs(share(b.simUptimeMs, sim) - share(b.actualUptimeMs, actual)) -
        Math.abs(share(a.simUptimeMs, sim) - share(a.actualUptimeMs, actual)),
    )
    .slice(0, MAX_AURA_LINES)
    .map(
      (row) =>
        `${row.name} uptime ${pct(row.actualUptimeMs, actual.duration_ms)}% against ${pct(row.simUptimeMs, sim.duration_ms)}%.`,
    );

  const headline =
    executionScore === null
      ? `This fight did ${round(actualDps)} DPS; the sim produced no damage to compare it with.`
      : `This fight did ${round(actualDps)} DPS; the sim expects ${round(simDps)}. That is ${Math.round(executionScore * 100)}% of what this gear can do.`;

  return {
    headline,
    simDps,
    actualDps,
    executionScore,
    abilities,
    auras,
    lines: [...castLines, ...auraLines],
  };
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write `CompareView.svelte`**

Render the six parts above in order. The headline is a `<p role="status">`; the lines are a `<ul class="flex flex-col gap-1">` of `<li class="text-[14px]">`; each table is a `bg-raised border border-line rounded-panel` with a `label text-muted` heading row that is `hidden md:grid`, and on phone each row carries its own two-value line the way `ActorRow` does. Every number is `font-mono tabular`. The full component is:

```svelte
<!-- web/src/components/sim/CompareView.svelte -->
<!-- The sim beside the fight. The words come first and the tables second, because most
     people will read "Bloodthirst cast 33 times, the sim expects 41" and stop -- that is
     the answer, and the tables are the working.
     Nothing here is coloured. A red-and-green diff would be a fourth colour vocabulary on
     a page that already has class colours, rarity colours and percentile tokens, and the
     design system does not allow colour to carry meaning on its own anyway. -->
<script lang="ts">
  import { simCopy } from '../../lib/sim/copy';
  import type { Comparison } from '../../lib/sim/compare';

  let {
    comparison,
    simDuration,
    actualDuration,
  }: { comparison: Comparison; simDuration: number; actualDuration: number } = $props();

  const pct = (ms: number, of: number): string => (of <= 0 ? '0%' : `${Math.round((ms / of) * 100)}%`);
  const amount = (value: number): string => Math.round(value).toLocaleString('en-US');

  const abilityRow =
    'border-line-soft grid min-h-11 grid-cols-[minmax(0,1fr)_auto_auto] items-center gap-x-3 gap-y-1 border-b px-3 py-2 text-[14px] last:border-b-0 md:grid-cols-[minmax(0,1.6fr)_72px_72px_88px_88px]';
</script>

<div class="flex flex-col gap-4" data-testid="sim-compare">
  <p class="text-strong px-[18px] text-[15px] md:px-0" role="status" data-testid="compare-headline">
    {comparison.headline}
  </p>

  {#if comparison.lines.length > 0}
    <ul class="flex flex-col gap-1 px-[18px] md:px-0" data-testid="compare-lines">
      {#each comparison.lines as line (line)}
        <li class="text-[14px]">{line}</li>
      {/each}
    </ul>
  {/if}

  <section class="border-line bg-raised rounded-panel mx-[18px] border md:mx-0" data-testid="compare-abilities">
    <div class={`${abilityRow} label text-muted hidden md:grid`}>
      <span>{simCopy.compareAbility}</span>
      <span class="text-right">{simCopy.compareActualCasts}</span>
      <span class="text-right">{simCopy.compareSimCasts}</span>
      <span class="text-right">{simCopy.compareActualDamage}</span>
      <span class="text-right">{simCopy.compareSimDamage}</span>
    </div>
    <!-- Keyed and tested by id, never by name: a resolved name can hold a space ("Heroic
         Strike"), a colon ("spell:25286") or a slash, none of which belong in a test id or
         in a Svelte key. -->
    {#each comparison.abilities as row (row.spellId)}
      <div class={abilityRow} data-testid={`compare-ability-${row.spellId}`}>
        <span class="text-strong truncate font-semibold">{row.name}</span>
        <span class="tabular text-right font-mono">{row.actualCasts}</span>
        <span class="tabular text-muted text-right font-mono">{row.simCasts}</span>
        <span class="tabular hidden text-right font-mono md:inline">{amount(row.actualDamage)}</span>
        <span class="tabular text-muted hidden text-right font-mono md:inline">{amount(row.simDamage)}</span>
        <span class="text-muted label col-span-3 md:hidden">
          {amount(row.actualDamage)} {simCopy.compareActual} · {amount(row.simDamage)} {simCopy.compareSimulated}
        </span>
      </div>
    {/each}
  </section>

  <section class="border-line bg-raised rounded-panel mx-[18px] border md:mx-0" data-testid="compare-auras">
    <div class="border-line-soft label text-muted grid min-h-11 grid-cols-[minmax(0,1fr)_72px_72px] items-center gap-x-3 border-b px-3 py-2">
      <span>{simCopy.compareBuff}</span>
      <span class="text-right">{simCopy.compareActual}</span>
      <span class="text-right">{simCopy.compareSimulated}</span>
    </div>
    {#each comparison.auras as row (row.name)}
      <div
        class="border-line-soft grid min-h-11 grid-cols-[minmax(0,1fr)_72px_72px] items-center gap-x-3 border-b px-3 py-2 text-[14px] last:border-b-0"
        data-testid={`compare-aura-${row.name}`}
      >
        <span class="text-strong truncate font-semibold">{row.name}</span>
        <span class="tabular text-right font-mono">{pct(row.actualUptimeMs, actualDuration)}</span>
        <span class="tabular text-muted text-right font-mono">{pct(row.simUptimeMs, simDuration)}</span>
      </div>
    {/each}
  </section>

  <p class="text-muted px-[18px] text-[12px] md:px-0" data-testid="compare-footnote">
    {simCopy.compareFootnote}
  </p>
</div>
```

with the copy:

- [ ] **Step 5: Wire it into `SimView.svelte`**

When `state.mode === 'compare'` and `state.ref` parses as a fight ref, `SimView` does this, in `SimView.svelte`'s script:

```ts
  import { fetchReportMeta, fetchSummary } from '../../lib/report/load';
  import { compareSummaries } from '../../lib/sim/compare';
  import { parseFightRef } from '../../lib/sim/sources';
  import type { Summary } from '../../lib/report/types';

  let actual = $state<Summary | null>(null);
  let comparing = $state(false);

  /**
   * Compare mode's whole entry path, called once from the same place the other `?source=`
   * loaders are called -- never from a `$effect`, which would re-fetch the fight whenever
   * anything unrelated in the store changed.
   */
  async function enterCompare(ref: string): Promise<void> {
    const parsed = parseFightRef(ref);
    if (parsed === null) {
      store.setMessage(simCopy.fightRefInvalid);
      return;
    }
    comparing = true;
    // loadFight gives us the character; fromLoggedFight is what it calls underneath.
    await store.loadFight(ref);
    try {
      const meta = await fetchReportMeta(parsed.reportId);
      actual = await fetchSummary(meta.data_base_url, parsed.fightIndex);
    } catch {
      // A dead page helps nobody: keep the character, drop back to plain sim mode.
      actual = null;
      comparing = false;
      store.setMessage(simCopy.fightNoCombatant);
      return;
    }
    // The one place a sim starts without a press: the player already pressed something to
    // get here, and a compare with nothing to compare against is not a page.
    if (store.character !== null) await store.run();
  }

  const comparison = $derived(
    comparing && actual !== null && store.result !== null && store.character !== null
      ? compareSummaries(store.result.summary, actual, store.character.name, store.actionNames)
      : null,
  );
```

and renders `<CompareView {comparison} simDuration={store.result?.summary.duration_ms ?? 0} actualDuration={actual?.duration_ms ?? 0} />` in place of the sentence and results whenever `comparison !== null`, showing `simCopy.compareLoading` while `comparing && actual === null`.

`store.setMessage(text)` is one line added to the store in this task (`message = text;`), so compare mode reports its two failures through the same `role="alert"` line every other source uses rather than growing a second error surface.

## Task 17: Saved sims — `/sim/<sim_id>` and the history list

Design 4.3: a public, shareable result with an unfurl card, stored like a build; signed-in players keep a history and can name entries.

**Files:**
- Create: `web/src/components/sim/SavedSim.svelte`, `web/src/components/sim/SimHistory.svelte`
- Modify: `web/src/components/sim/SimView.svelte`, `web/src/lib/sim/copy.ts`, `web/src/lib/sim/store.svelte.ts`
- Modify: `web/src/components/sim/CharacterStrip.svelte` (Step 2 adds the talent-line rule for a build with no point order)
- Test: `web/tests/e2e/sim-saved.spec.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `fetchSim`, `saveSim`, `listMySims` (Task 2); `SimResult`, `SimListRow` (Task 2); `isStale`, `engineLabel` (Task 1); `settingsLabel`, `encounterLabel` (Task 6); `specLabel` (Task 1); `SimResults` (Task 14); `CharacterStrip` (Task 11).
- Produces:
  - `SavedSim.svelte`: `let { result, onrerun }: { result: SimResult; onrerun: () => void } = $props();`
  - `SimHistory.svelte`: `let { rows, error, onopen }: { rows: SimListRow[] | null; error: string | null; onopen: (simId: string) => void } = $props();`
  - `store.save()` extended to take an optional title; `store.rename(simId, title)` is **not** added — see below.

### Saving, decided

The `Save this sim` button under the results:

1. is disabled until `store.result !== null`;
2. on press, opens a one-field inline form, not a dialog: a text input pre-filled with `settingsLabel(store.settings)` — `Raid-buffed, 3:00, single target` — a `Save` button and a `Cancel` button. Pre-filling means the common case is one more press, and the field is what the contract's `title` column holds;
3. on success, replaces the form with the saved URL in a read-only input and a `Copy link` button, exactly as `SharePanel.svelte` already does for builds. The page does **not** navigate: the player is looking at their result and moving them away from it to show them the same result is a worse page.
4. on failure, shows `simCopy.saveFailed` beside the button and leaves the form open with what they typed.

Renaming a saved sim is **not** built. The contract has no route for it (`POST /v1/sims` and four reads, no `PATCH`), and inventing one is the API lane's call, not this lane's. The design's "can name entries" is satisfied by naming at save time. **Record this in the finish report.**

### `/sim/<sim_id>`, decided

A saved sim is read-only and is not the sim page with a result in it. What renders, top to bottom:

1. The heading row, with the title as the `<h1>` when the sim has one, and `specLabel(spec)` when it does not.
2. A **result header** in place of the run control: one `bg-raised border border-line rounded-panel p-4` panel, `flex flex-wrap items-baseline gap-x-6 gap-y-2`, carrying
   - the figure at `text-[32px] font-mono tabular text-gold` with its `± band`,
   - `encounterLabel(...)` in `text-muted text-[13px]`,
   - `3,000 iterations · 5.1 s · browser` in mono muted,
   - `engineLabel(result.engine_version)` as a `pill pill-sample` when stale, plain mono text when current,
   - the saved date.
3. The character strip, built from `result.request.character` — **not** from `result.summary.roster[0]`. `CharacterStrip`'s prop is a `SimCharacter` (Task 11) and a `RosterRow` is a different shape with different field names; the request is where the character actually lives, and it is stored whole (`sim/api`: "Request is stored whole. There is nothing to strip"). The conversion is four lines and belongs in `SavedSim.svelte`:

```ts
  /** The stored request back into the strip's own shape. Every field is already there. */
  const character = $derived<SimCharacter>({
    name: result.request.character.name,
    spec: result.request.spec,
    class_slug: result.request.character.class,
    race_slug: result.request.character.race,
    talent_level: SIM_LEVEL,
    tree_version: activeBuild.build,
    // The stored gear is the engine's list, not the planner's map; the strip only needs
    // item ids per slot and that is exactly what a GearSlot carries.
    point_order: [],
    gear: Object.fromEntries(result.request.character.gear.map((slot) => [slot.slot, slot.item_id])),
    buffs: [...result.request.character.buffs],
    consumables: [...result.request.character.consumes],
    source: result.request.source,
  });
```

`point_order` is empty because a stored request carries the engine's talent **string**, not the planner's order, and the strip shows a split rather than an order — so the strip takes `gearKnown` as well, and with `point_order` empty it renders the split from `result.request.character.talents` instead. The gear grid still renders: the item ids are all there. What is not there is the item **file** for a class the page learns only from the result, so the grid draws slot names and ids without icons or rarities until `loadItems` resolves, exactly as it does on `/sim` before the fetch lands.
4. `SimResults`, unchanged.
5. Two actions: `Run this yourself`, which opens `/sim` with the request's source and ref in the query so the player can re-run and change things, and `Copy link`.

A stale engine version shows the pill and `simCopy.staleEngine`, and `Run this yourself` is the offered remedy. It is never re-run automatically.

### The history list

On `/sim` for a signed-in player, below the source switcher (so it does not push the switcher off the fold): a `Your sims` panel listing up to 100 rows from `GET /v1/sims?mine=1`, each a `min-h-11` row of `title · specLabel · 1,131 DPS · date`, the whole row a link to `/sim/<id>`. Signed out, the panel does not render at all — not a signed-out placeholder, which would be an advertisement on a page that is otherwise useful without an account.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write `SavedSim.svelte`**

Compose the result header described above from `result`, and render `CharacterStrip` with the `character` derived above and `gearKnown={result.request.character.gear.length > 0}`. Add the two actions. Every number is `font-mono tabular`; the date is `result.request.source.captured_at`'s day, and an absent one is not invented.

- [ ] **Step 2: Extend `CharacterStrip.svelte` for the gearless case**

`gearKnown` is already on the props, with the `simCopy.savedNoGear` branch in place of the grid: Task 11 wrote both (its own strip has a `{#if !gearKnown}`), because the prop and the branch are one edit and splitting them across two tasks would leave Task 11's component with a prop nothing reads. What this task adds is the **talent line**: render the split from `character.point_order` only when it has points, and nothing when it is empty, so a saved sim — whose stored request carries the engine's talent string rather than the planner's order — does not claim a 0-point build. `simCopy.savedNoGear` is Task 11's, appended with the rest of the strip's copy, because Task 11 is what renders it.

## Task 18: The signed-in landing state

Design 4.6: "`/sim` for a member shows their characters with last-logout gear and one button per character. No form is shown unless the member opens one."

**Files:**
- Create: `web/src/components/sim/LandingState.svelte`
- Modify: `web/src/components/sim/SimView.svelte`, `web/src/components/sim/SourceSwitcher.svelte`, `web/src/lib/sim/copy.ts`
- Test: `web/tests/e2e/sim-landing.spec.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `fetchMe`, `Me`, `MeCharacter` from `web/src/lib/account/api.ts`; `fromStoredCharacter` (Task 7); `characterKey`, `CharacterPath` from `web/src/lib/characters.ts`; `classColorVar` from `web/src/lib/report/format.ts`.
- Produces: `LandingState.svelte`: `let { characters, busyKey, onpick, onother }: { characters: MeCharacter[]; busyKey: string | null; onpick: (path: CharacterPath) => void; onother: () => void } = $props();`

### Decided, including the limitation

For a signed-in member with characters, `/sim` opens on the landing state **instead of** the source switcher:

- One `bg-raised border border-line rounded-panel` list, one row per character: a 28px class-coloured square, the name in the class colour, a muted `Whitemane · US` descriptor, and a right-aligned `Sim` button at `min-h-11`. The whole row is not the button — the row is a link to `/sim?source=armory&ref=<key>`, and the button is the same action; a member on a phone gets a 44px target either way.
- Below the list, one plain text button: `simCopy.otherCharacter`, "Sim something else" — which opens the source switcher. That is the design's "no form is shown unless the member opens one".
- While a character is loading, its own row's button reads `Loading…` and is disabled; the others stay live.

**The limitation, stated on the page.** Per the contract, Armory is not a source yet: `sim-input` answers from the newest addon export or last ranked fight. So the list's own footnote is `simCopy.landingSourceNote` — *"Gear comes from your last addon export or your last logged fight. Blizzard has no character profile API for Forever yet."* — and each row's source pill, once loaded, says which of the two it actually was, because `fromStoredCharacter` copies the API's answer. The design's phrase "last-logout gear" is exactly what an addon export is, so this is the design honoured through the only source that exists, not a substitute for it.

**The second limitation, and it is louder.** `sim-input` records no race, and `fromStoredCharacter` refuses rather than substituting one (Task 7). So until the API sends `race`, picking a character here answers with `simCopy.unknownRace` and the page must say what to do instead: the row's failure message is followed by `simCopy.landingNoRace` — *"Paste your addon export instead; it carries your race."* — and the `Sim something else` button below the list opens the switcher on the addon field. The e2e covers both the success path (the stub sends a race) and this one. **Do not make the landing state guess a race to avoid this state.**

A member with **no** characters gets the source switcher and one line: `simCopy.noCharactersYet`, *"No characters yet. Install the addon and the companion, or paste an export."* with a link to `/logs`.

`fetchMe` is called once by `SimView` on mount; a null answer is the signed-out state and is not an error.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the component**

```svelte
<!-- web/src/components/sim/LandingState.svelte -->
<!-- What a signed-in member sees when they open /sim: their characters, one button each,
     and no form at all until they ask for one.
     The footnote is not boilerplate. The contract's sim-input has no Armory source yet, so
     the gear behind each of these rows is the member's last addon export or last logged
     fight, and a member who assumed the Armory was being read would be wrong about how
     fresh their gear is. The design's "last-logout gear" is precisely what an addon export
     holds, so this is the feature through the source that exists. -->
<script lang="ts">
  import type { MeCharacter } from '../../lib/account/api';
  import type { CharacterPath } from '../../lib/characters';
  import { rulesetLabel } from '../../lib/characters';
  import { classColorVar } from '../../lib/report/format';
  import { simCopy } from '../../lib/sim/copy';

  let {
    characters,
    busyKey,
    onpick,
    onother,
  }: {
    characters: MeCharacter[];
    busyKey: string | null;
    onpick: (path: CharacterPath) => void;
    onother: () => void;
  } = $props();

  function pathOf(character: MeCharacter): CharacterPath {
    const [region, ruleset, slug] = character.key.split('/');
    return { region, ruleset, slug } as CharacterPath;
  }
</script>

<section class="mx-[18px] flex flex-col gap-3 md:mx-0" data-testid="sim-landing">
  <h2 class="section-title text-[15px]">{simCopy.yourCharacters}</h2>

  <ul class="border-line bg-raised rounded-panel flex flex-col border">
    {#each characters as character (character.key)}
      {@const colour = classColorVar(character.class)}
      <li
        class="border-line-soft flex min-h-11 flex-wrap items-center gap-3 border-b px-3 py-2 last:border-b-0"
        data-testid={`sim-character-${character.key}`}
      >
        <span class="rounded-control h-7 w-7 shrink-0" style={`background: ${colour}`} aria-hidden="true"></span>
        <span class="text-[15px] font-semibold" style={`color: ${colour}`}>{character.name}</span>
        <span class="text-muted text-[13px]">
          {rulesetLabel(character.ruleset)} · {character.region.toUpperCase()}
        </span>
        <button
          type="button"
          class="border-line-warm-strong rounded-control text-strong label ml-auto min-h-11 border px-4 disabled:opacity-50 md:min-h-9"
          disabled={busyKey !== null}
          onclick={() => onpick(pathOf(character))}
          data-testid={`sim-pick-${character.key}`}
        >
          {busyKey === character.key ? simCopy.loading : simCopy.simIt}
        </button>
      </li>
    {/each}
  </ul>

  <p class="text-muted text-[12px]" data-testid="sim-landing-note">{simCopy.landingSourceNote}</p>

  <button
    type="button"
    class="text-nav label min-h-11 self-start underline md:min-h-9"
    onclick={onother}
    data-testid="sim-other-character"
  >
    {simCopy.otherCharacter}
  </button>
</section>
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Wire it into `SimView.svelte`**

`SimView` calls `fetchMe()` once on mount into `me`. Then, when no character is loaded:

- `me !== null && me.characters.length > 0 && !switcherOpen` → `LandingState`;
- `me !== null && me.characters.length === 0` → `SourceSwitcher` with `signedIn` and the `noCharactersYet` line above it;
- otherwise → `SourceSwitcher` with `signedIn={false}`.

`onother` sets `switcherOpen = true`; `SourceSwitcher`'s account card, when `signedIn`, renders a `Back to your characters` button instead of the sign-in button.

Premium comes straight from the session: `GET /v1/me` returns it as `user.premium`, so `SimView` calls `store.setPremium(me?.user.premium === true)` in the same effect that reads `me`. A signed-out visitor is `null` and therefore not premium, which is the correct default and needs no special case. **`premium` is already on `Me['user']`**: Task 13 added it, because the server-lane control is the first thing that reads it and Task 13 is earlier in group D. This task adds nothing to `web/src/lib/account/api.ts`.

## Task 19: The phone layout audit

Every component in this group was written with its phone layout. This task proves it, the way `report-phone.spec.ts` proves the report's.

**Files:**
- Create: `web/tests/e2e/sim-phone.spec.ts`
- Modify: whichever components the audit catches
- Modify: `web/package.json`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: everything in Group D.
- Produces: `sim-phone.spec.ts` **appended** to the existing `test:e2e:phone` script, which already names four phone suites: `"test:e2e:phone": "playwright test report-phone.spec.ts rankings-phone.spec.ts character-phone.spec.ts guild-phone.spec.ts sim-phone.spec.ts --project=mobile"`. Do not rewrite the list; the other four are the report, rankings, character and guild phone audits and dropping them would silently stop running them.

### What the audit checks

**At the Pixel 7's 412×915**, which is what `--project=mobile` runs and what the Global Constraints name (~400 px). The script that runs this file passes `--project=mobile`, so a `test.use({ viewport })` of some other size would silently audit a width nobody tests at; the file therefore sets **no** viewport of its own and inherits the project's. The gutter arithmetic below is against `page.viewportSize()`, not a literal, for the same reason.

1. **No horizontal scroll, anywhere.** `document.documentElement.scrollWidth <= window.innerWidth` on `/sim` empty, `/sim` with a character, `/sim` after a run on every one of the six result tabs, `/sim` in compare mode, `/sim/specs`, and `/sim/simfixtureab`.
2. **Every target is 44px.** Every visible `button`, `select`, `a[href]`, `input` and `textarea` not inside a `<p>` has `max(height, width) >= 44`.
3. **The gutter never closes.** Every section's left edge is at `x >= GUTTER` and its right edge at `x <= width - GUTTER`, with `width` read from `page.viewportSize()`.
4. **The run control reaches the figure without scrolling.** After loading a character, `sim-run-button` and `sim-dps` are both `toBeInViewport()` — a player on a phone must be able to press run and see the number without moving.
5. **The results tab strip scrolls rather than wraps.** `sim-tab-distribution` is reachable by `scrollIntoViewIfNeeded` and the strip's own `scrollWidth` exceeds its client width; the page's does not.
6. **The gear grid is two columns.** `SLOTS` on `main` begins head, neck, shoulder, so in two columns head and neck share row 1 and shoulder starts row 2. The assertion is exactly that: `y(sim-slot-head) === y(sim-slot-neck)`, `x(sim-slot-head) < x(sim-slot-neck)`, and `y(sim-slot-shoulder) > y(sim-slot-head)`. (The earlier draft of this item said head and shoulder were the same row *and* head and neck were; both cannot be true in two columns, and `SLOTS` settles which.)
7. **Compare's ability rows stack their two figures**, checked by asserting the phone label span inside `compare-ability-25286` is visible and the desktop damage cells are not. The test id is `compare-ability-${row.spellId}` — the row's own integer identity, never its name, which can hold a space or a colon and which no `slug()` exists to clean. `25286` is Heroic Strike's client spell id and is the compare fixture's top row (Task 16); if that row moves, read the id from the rendered element rather than hardcoding a different one.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the audit**

Create `web/tests/e2e/sim-phone.spec.ts`, modelled on `web/tests/e2e/report-phone.spec.ts` — reuse its helper shape rather than inventing a second one:

```ts
import { expect, test, type Page } from '@playwright/test';

test.describe.configure({ mode: 'serial' });
// No test.use({ viewport }): the phone audit runs under --project=mobile (Pixel 7, 412 px),
// which is the width the Global Constraints name, and overriding it here would audit a
// size nothing else in the suite uses.

const GUTTER = 18;
const TARGET = 44;

async function noHorizontalScroll(page: Page): Promise<void> {
  const overflow = await page.evaluate(
    () => document.documentElement.scrollWidth - window.innerWidth,
  );
  expect(overflow, 'page scrolls horizontally').toBeLessThanOrEqual(0);
}

async function targetsAreBigEnough(page: Page): Promise<void> {
  const small = await page.evaluate(
    ({ target }) =>
      [...document.querySelectorAll('button, select, a[href], input, textarea')]
        .filter((element) => {
          const box = element.getBoundingClientRect();
          return box.width > 0 && box.height > 0 && element.closest('p') === null;
        })
        .filter((element) => {
          const box = element.getBoundingClientRect();
          return Math.max(box.height, box.width) < target;
        })
        .map((element) => `${element.tagName}:${element.textContent?.trim().slice(0, 30) ?? ''}`),
    { target: TARGET },
  );
  expect(small, 'hit targets under 44px').toEqual([]);
}
```

then one test per page state calling both helpers, plus the five specific checks above.

# Group F — the planner surfaces

Tasks 20 and 21, in order. They depend on groups A and B, and on nothing in C, D or E: the planner is a different page with a different island.

## Task 20: Live DPS in the planner

Design 4.6: "A talent or slot change in the planner runs a 500-iteration sim in the browser pool and updates a DPS estimate with its error in under a second; a 'Sim this build' control opens the full results."

**Files:**
- Create: `web/src/lib/planner/live-dps.svelte.ts`, `web/src/components/planner/PlannerDps.svelte`
- Modify: `web/src/components/planner/Planner.svelte`, `web/src/components/planner/SummaryBar.svelte`, `web/src/lib/sim/copy.ts`
- Test: `web/src/lib/planner/live-dps.test.ts`, `web/tests/e2e/planner-dps.spec.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `PlannerStore` from `web/src/lib/planner/store.svelte.ts`; `createPool`, `SimPool` (Task 4); `runSim`, `SimRunError` (Task 5); `specOf`, `SimCharacter` (Task 6); `ITERATIONS` (Task 2); `confidenceBand` (Task 4); `defaultSettings` (Task 6).
- Produces:
  - `web/src/lib/planner/live-dps.svelte.ts`: `const LIVE_DEBOUNCE_MS = 350`, `type LiveState = 'off' | 'pending' | 'running' | 'ready' | 'error'`, `function createLiveDps(init?: { pool?: SimPool; debounceMs?: number }): LiveDps` returning `{ readonly state: LiveState; readonly estimate: Estimate; readonly iterationsRun: number; readonly message: string | null; request(character: SimCharacter | null, index: TalentIndex | null): void; dispose(): void }`, `type LiveDps = ReturnType<typeof createLiveDps>` (the runes-based factory's own return type, so the two can never disagree — Task 21 and `PlannerDps.svelte` both annotate against it), `function characterFromPlanner(store: PlannerStore): SimCharacter | null`. There is **no** `now` option: nothing here reads a clock, the debounce is `setTimeout`, and the tests drive it with `vi.useFakeTimers()`.
  - `PlannerDps.svelte`: `let { live, href }: { live: LiveDps; href: string } = $props();`

### The rule that protects the planner's Lighthouse budget

`/planner.html` has a performance budget of 0.90 and a total-blocking-time budget of 100 ms in `lighthouserc.json`. A 4 MB wasm module fetched on page load would end both. So:

- **The pool is created on the planner's first edit, never on mount.** `createLiveDps` holds `pool: SimPool | null` and calls `createPool` inside `request()`, not in the factory.
- **Nothing in the planner's module graph imports `engine.ts` statically.** `live-dps.svelte.ts` imports `runSim` and `createPool`; `createPool`'s worker is `new Worker(new URL('./sim.worker.ts', …))`, which Vite emits as its own chunk, and `sim.worker.ts` is the only file that imports `engine.ts`. Task 22 asserts this by checking that `dist/planner-island.js` does not grow past its 60 KB budget, which it would if the engine were inlined.
- **A change while a sim is running cancels it.** Every edit calls `request()`; `request()` clears the pending timer, cancels the in-flight handle, and schedules a new run 350 ms later. Without the cancel, a player dragging through five talents would queue five 500-iteration runs and the pool would be busy for seconds.

350 ms is the debounce: long enough that a burst of clicks is one run, short enough that a deliberate single change feels immediate. 500 iterations at the measured 155 iterations per second per worker is about 0.8 s on a four-worker laptop, which is the design's "under a second".

### The display, decided

A fourth figure in `SummaryBar.svelte`, beside Level, Split and Points, in exactly the same shape — `label text-muted` caption, `font-mono tabular text-[20px] leading-11` value:

| State | Caption | Value |
|---|---|---|
| `off` (no engine yet, no edit made) | `DPS` | `—` in `text-muted` |
| `pending` (debouncing) | `DPS` | the last figure, dimmed to `text-muted` |
| `running` | `DPS` | the last figure, dimmed |
| `ready` | `DPS` | the figure in `text-gold`, with `± band` in `text-muted text-[12px]` under it |
| `error` | `DPS` | `—`, and the message in the bar's existing refusal line |

The figure is never blanked while a new one is computed: a number that vanishes and reappears on every click is worse than a stale number that dims. That is the whole design decision here.

Beside it, a `Sim this build` link at `min-h-11`, to `/sim?source=build&ref=<id>` when the build has been saved, and to `/sim` with the FS1 code in `?code=` otherwise — using the planner's own `encodeFS1`, so no new format. The link is always present, so a player can reach the full results whether or not the estimate has run.

An unsupported spec shows `—` and keeps the link. The planner does **not** fetch `/v1/specs` to find out which specs those are: a fetch on the planner's critical path is exactly what its Lighthouse budget forbids, and the engine is the authority anyway — a spec it has no model for fails the run, `live.state` becomes `error`, and `simCopy.liveDpsFailed` goes in the summary bar's existing refusal line. The `Sim this build` link is still there, and `/sim` has the full spec card for anyone who wants the reason.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `web/src/lib/planner/live-dps.test.ts`:

```ts
// @vitest-environment jsdom
import { readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
import path from 'node:path';
import { afterEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest';
import { indexTalents, type TalentIndex } from './rules';
import type { TalentFile } from './types';
import { createFakeEngine } from '../../fixtures/sim/engine-fake';
import { createFakeWorker } from '../../test-support/fake-worker';
import { createPool, type FromWorker, type PoolWorker, type ToWorker } from '../sim/worker';
import type { SimCharacter } from '../sim/character';
import { LIVE_DEBOUNCE_MS, createLiveDps } from './live-dps.svelte';

const character: SimCharacter = {
  name: 'Thrallgar',
  spec: 'warrior-fury',
  class_slug: 'warrior',
  race_slug: 'orc',
  talent_level: 60,
  tree_version: '1.15.9.69722',
  point_order: [2001, 2002],
  gear: { head: 12640 },
  buffs: [],
  consumables: [],
  source: { kind: 'manual', ref: '', captured_at: '2026-09-14T10:00:00Z' },
};

/** The shared in-process worker (Task 3), wrapped so each test names its own tick rate. */
function fakeWorker(tickMs = 0): PoolWorker {
  return createFakeWorker(createFakeEngine({ tickMs, ticks: 3 }));
}

const live = () => createLiveDps({ pool: createPool({ hardwareConcurrency: 2, spawn: fakeWorker }) });

/** The fixture warrior's talent index; the engine's talents string is built from it. */
async function warriorIndex(): Promise<TalentIndex> {
  const file = JSON.parse(
    await readFile(
      path.join(import.meta.dirname, '../../../public/data/1.15.9.69722/talents/warrior.json'),
      'utf8',
    ),
  ) as TalentFile;
  return indexTalents(file);
}

afterEach(() => vi.useRealTimers());

describe('createLiveDps', () => {
  it('is off until something is requested', () => {
    const dps = live();
    expect(dps.state).toBe('off');
    expect(dps.estimate.mean).toBe(0);
    dps.dispose();
  });

  it('debounces a burst of edits into one run', async () => {
    const index = await warriorIndex();
    vi.useFakeTimers();
    const dps = live();
    dps.request(character, index);
    dps.request(character, index);
    dps.request(character, index);
    expect(dps.state).toBe('pending');
    await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(LIVE_DEBOUNCE_MS - 1);
    expect(dps.state).toBe('pending');
    await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(50);
    await vi.runAllTimersAsync();
    expect(dps.state).toBe('ready');
    expect(dps.estimate.mean).toBeGreaterThan(0);
    dps.dispose();
  });

  it('runs 500 iterations, which is what "under a second" buys', async () => {
    const index = await warriorIndex();
    vi.useFakeTimers();
    const dps = live();
    dps.request(character, index);
    await vi.runAllTimersAsync();
    expect(dps.iterationsRun).toBe(500);
    dps.dispose();
  });

  it('keeps the last figure while the next one is computed', async () => {
    const index = await warriorIndex();
    vi.useFakeTimers();
    const dps = live();
    dps.request(character, index);
    await vi.runAllTimersAsync();
    const first = dps.estimate.mean;
    dps.request({ ...character, gear: { head: 12641 } }, index);
    expect(dps.state).toBe('pending');
    expect(dps.estimate.mean).toBe(first);
    dps.dispose();
  });

  it('goes off, not to an error, when there is no character to sim', async () => {
    const index = await warriorIndex();
    const dps = live();
    dps.request(null, index);
    expect(dps.state).toBe('off');
    dps.dispose();
  });

  it('says so rather than throwing when the engine fails', async () => {
    const index = await warriorIndex();
    vi.useFakeTimers();
    let emit: (data: FromWorker) => void = () => {};
    const broken: PoolWorker = {
      addEventListener: (_type, listener) => {
        emit = (data) => listener({ data });
      },
      terminate: () => {},
      postMessage: (message: ToWorker) => {
        if (message.kind === 'abort') return;
        emit({ kind: 'failed', token: message.token, message: 'wasm trap' });
      },
    };
    const dps = createLiveDps({ pool: createPool({ hardwareConcurrency: 1, spawn: () => broken }) });
    dps.request(character, index);
    await vi.runAllTimersAsync();
    expect(dps.state).toBe('error');
    expect(dps.message).not.toBeNull();
    dps.dispose();
  });
});
```
