Expected: FAIL. Then create `web/src/lib/planner/live-dps.svelte.ts`:

```ts
// web/src/lib/planner/live-dps.svelte.ts
// A 500-iteration sim behind every planner edit, and the three rules that make it usable.
//
//   1. The pool is created on the first request, never in this factory. /planner.html has a
//      0.90 performance budget and a 100 ms total-blocking-time budget in lighthouserc.json;
//      a 4 MB wasm module on page load ends both. Nothing on the planner's critical path may
//      reach engine.ts, which is why sim.worker.ts -- a separate Vite chunk -- is the only
//      file that imports it.
//   2. A burst of edits is one run. Clicking through five talents must not queue five runs.
//   3. The last figure stays on screen, dimmed, while the next one is computed. A number
//      that blanks and reappears on every click is worse than a stale number that dims.
import type { TalentIndex } from './rules';
import { EMPTY_ESTIMATE } from '../sim/estimate';
import { runSim, SimRunError, type RunHandle } from '../sim/run';
import { defaultSettings } from '../sim/settings';
import { toCharacterSpec, type SimCharacter } from '../sim/character';
import { simCopy } from '../sim/copy';
import { ITERATIONS, type Estimate } from '../sim/types';
import { createPool, type SimPool } from '../sim/worker';

export const LIVE_DEBOUNCE_MS = 350;

export type LiveState = 'off' | 'pending' | 'running' | 'ready' | 'error';

export function createLiveDps(init: { pool?: SimPool; debounceMs?: number } = {}) {
  const debounceMs = init.debounceMs ?? LIVE_DEBOUNCE_MS;

  let state = $state<LiveState>('off');
  let estimate = $state<Estimate>(EMPTY_ESTIMATE);
  let iterationsRun = $state(0);
  let message = $state<string | null>(null);

  let pool: SimPool | null = init.pool ?? null;
  let handle: RunHandle | null = null;
  let timer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null = null;

  function poolOnce(): SimPool {
    pool ??= createPool({});
    return pool;
  }

  async function start(character: SimCharacter, index: TalentIndex): Promise<void> {
    state = 'running';
    const settings = defaultSettings();
    handle = runSim(
      poolOnce(),
      {
        spec: character.spec,
        source: character.source,
        character: toCharacterSpec(character, index, settings.buffs, settings.consumables),
        encounter: settings.encounter,
        iterations: ITERATIONS.live,
      },
      () => {
        // The planner shows one figure at the end, not a ticking one: a number that moves
        // while a player is reading their talent tree is noise, and 500 iterations is fast
        // enough that there is nothing to fill.
      },
    );

    try {
      const result = await handle.result;
      estimate = result.dps;
      iterationsRun = result.iterations_run;
      message = null;
      state = 'ready';
    } catch (error) {
      // A cancel is this module's own doing -- a newer edit arrived -- and is not a failure
      // the player should be told about; the newer run will set the state.
      if (error instanceof SimRunError && error.cancelled) return;
      message = simCopy.liveDpsFailed;
      state = 'error';
    } finally {
      handle = null;
    }
  }

  return {
    get state() {
      return state;
    },
    get estimate() {
      return estimate;
    },
    get iterationsRun() {
      return iterationsRun;
    },
    get message() {
      return message;
    },

    request(character: SimCharacter | null, index: TalentIndex | null): void {
      if (timer !== null) clearTimeout(timer);
      handle?.cancel();
      if (character === null || index === null) {
        state = 'off';
        return;
      }
      state = 'pending';
      timer = setTimeout(() => {
        timer = null;
        void start(character, index);
      }, debounceMs);
    },

    dispose(): void {
      if (timer !== null) clearTimeout(timer);
      timer = null;
      handle?.cancel();
      pool?.terminate();
      pool = null;
    },
  };
}

export type LiveDps = ReturnType<typeof createLiveDps>;
```

and add `liveDpsFailed: 'DPS estimate unavailable for this build.'` to `simCopy`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `characterFromPlanner`**

Add to `web/src/lib/sim/character.ts` — it belongs with the other conversions, not in the planner:

```ts
/**
 * The planner's live state as a character. It is `fromBuildDraft` over `store.toDraft()`,
 * so there is exactly one conversion in the codebase and the sim cannot disagree with the
 * planner about what a build is. Null while the planner's data is still loading.
 */
export function characterFromPlanner(store: PlannerStore): SimCharacter | null {
  if (store.talents === null || store.classes.length === 0) return null;
  return fromBuildDraft(store.toDraft(), store.talents, store.classes, store.races, {
    name: store.classRow?.name,
    source: { kind: 'manual', ref: '', captured_at: new Date().toISOString() },
  });
}
```

```svelte
<!-- web/src/components/planner/PlannerDps.svelte -->
<!-- The fourth figure in the summary bar. It is the planner's own shape -- label caption,
     mono value at the same size -- so it reads as one more fact about the build rather
     than a widget bolted on.
     It dims rather than blanks while a new estimate runs: a number that disappears on every

    live.state === 'ready' && live.estimate.error > 0
      ? `± ${Math.round(confidenceBand(live.estimate)).toLocaleString('en-US')}`
      : '',
  );
</script>

    data-testid="planner-dps"
  >
    {figure}
  </span>
  {#if band !== ''}
    <span class="tabular text-muted font-mono text-[12px]" data-testid="planner-dps-error">{band}</span>
  {/if}
</div>

<a class="label text-nav flex min-h-11 items-center underline md:min-h-9" {href} data-testid="planner-sim-link">
  {simCopy.simThisBuild}
</a>
```

## Task 21: Build unfurls carrying simmed DPS

Design 4.7: "A shared planner link's card carries the build's simmed DPS and the engine version it was simmed on."

**Files:**
- Modify: `web/src/components/planner/SharePanel.svelte`, `web/src/lib/sim/copy.ts`
- Test: `web/tests/e2e/planner-share-sim.spec.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `saveBuild`, `SavedBuild` from `web/src/lib/planner/share.ts`; `PlannerStore` from `web/src/lib/planner/store.svelte.ts`; `saveSim` (Task 2); `ITERATIONS` (Task 2); `simCopy` (Task 2); `LiveDps`, `characterFromPlanner` (Task 20); `runSim` (Task 5); `createPool`, `SimPool` (Task 4); `toCharacterSpec` (Task 6); `defaultSettings` (Task 6). **Not** `buildSimRequest`: `runSim` builds the envelope from its `RunInput`, and calling both would build it twice.
- Produces: no new module. `SharePanel.svelte` gains `live` and `store` props and one extra step in its save flow.

### The ruling this task makes, and why it changes no contract

The card is rendered by the API. The design says it carries the build's simmed DPS; the contract adds no field to `BuildDraft` and no field to the card route. Rather than invent one:

**When a build is saved and the planner has a live estimate for exactly that build, the share panel also saves a sim for it: a 3,000-iteration browser run posted to `POST /v1/sims` with `source: { kind: 'build', ref: <the new build id>, captured_at: <now> }`.** The API's card renderer then finds the newest sim whose `source.kind` is `build` and whose `source.ref` is that build id, and puts its `dps.mean` and `engine_version` on the card. That is a join over two shapes the contract already defines, and it needs no new field anywhere. **Record it in the finish report as a cross-lane dependency: the API lane owns the card side.**

Three consequences the panel must handle, and does:

1. The 500-iteration live estimate is **not** what is saved. It is a rough figure for a planner; a card is a public claim. The panel runs a fresh 3,000-iteration sim, which is why saving a build with a sim takes a few seconds and the button says so.
2. The sim is saved **after** the build, because it needs the build's id.
3. A failed sim never fails the save. The build is already saved and its link already works; the panel shows `simCopy.buildSimSkipped` and the card simply carries no DPS. Losing a share because a background sim failed would be the worst possible trade.

### The panel, decided

Under the existing `Save and share` button, one checkbox: `Include a simmed DPS on the card`, checked by default when the planner's live estimate is `ready` and the spec is supported, unchecked and disabled otherwise with the title `simCopy.buildSimUnavailable`.

While the sim runs after a save, the share URL is already shown and a muted line under it reads `simCopy.buildSimRunning` — "Simming this build for the card…". It becomes `simCopy.buildSimDone(dps, version)` — "The card will show 1,131 DPS on engine df3b36e4c." — or `simCopy.buildSimSkipped` — "The card will not show a DPS figure; the sim did not finish." The link is usable throughout.

- [ ] **Step 2: Extend `SharePanel.svelte`**

Add `live: LiveDps` and `store: PlannerStore` to its props, the checkbox described above, and after a successful `saveBuild`:

Every identifier the flow touches is declared in the component's own script — nothing here is implied:

```svelte
<script lang="ts">
  import { saveBuild, type SavedBuild } from '../../lib/planner/share';
  import type { PlannerStore } from '../../lib/planner/store.svelte';
  import { characterFromPlanner, type LiveDps } from '../../lib/planner/live-dps.svelte';
  import { saveSim } from '../../lib/sim/api';
  import { toCharacterSpec } from '../../lib/sim/character';
  import { simCopy } from '../../lib/sim/copy';
  import { runSim } from '../../lib/sim/run';
  import { defaultSettings } from '../../lib/sim/settings';
  import { ITERATIONS } from '../../lib/sim/types';
  import { createPool, type SimPool } from '../../lib/sim/worker';

  let { store, live }: { store: PlannerStore; live: LiveDps } = $props();

  /** Checked by default when the planner has an estimate for this build; see the panel. */
  let includeSim = $state(true);
  let cardState = $state<'idle' | 'running' | 'done' | 'skipped'>('idle');
  let cardDps = $state('');
  let cardVersion = $state('');

  // The card sim runs at the settings the sim page opens on -- raid-buffed, three minutes,
  // single target. The planner has no settings bar of its own, and inventing one here
  // would put two vocabularies on one page; `/sim` is where settings are chosen.
  const settings = defaultSettings();

  /**
   * One pool for the life of the panel, created on the first save and not before: the
   * planner's own Lighthouse budget is the reason the engine is never touched on mount.
   * `live` already holds one for the inline estimate, but it is private to that factory
   * and a shared handle would couple two features that cancel each other's runs.
   */
  let pool: SimPool | null = null;
  function poolOnce(): SimPool {
    pool ??= createPool();
    return pool;
  }

  async function attachSim(build: SavedBuild): Promise<void> {
    const character = characterFromPlanner(store);
    if (!includeSim || character === null || store.talentIndex === null) return;
    cardState = 'running';
    try {
      const result = await runSim(
        poolOnce(),
        {
          spec: character.spec,
          // The saved build is what the card is for, so that is the sim's source and the
          // id the API's card renderer joins on. No new field on either shape.
          source: { kind: 'build', ref: build.id, captured_at: new Date().toISOString() },
          character: toCharacterSpec(character, store.talentIndex, settings.buffs, settings.consumables),
          encounter: settings.encounter,
          iterations: ITERATIONS.normal,
        },
        () => {},
      ).result;
      await saveSim(result);
      cardDps = Math.round(result.dps.mean).toLocaleString('en-US');
      cardVersion = result.engine_version;
      cardState = 'done';
    } catch {
      // The build is saved and its link works. A background sim is not worth losing that.
      cardState = 'skipped';
    }
  }

  $effect(() => () => pool?.terminate());
</script>
```

`store.talentIndex` is checked rather than asserted with `!`: it is null until the class's talent file loads, and a non-null assertion on a value that is legitimately null is how a page throws in front of a player.

In `web/lighthouserc.json`, add all three sim URLs to `collect.url`:

```json

```

and add one matrix entry for the two shells:

```json
        {
          "matchingUrlPattern": ".*/sim(?:/specs)?\\.html$",
          "aggregationMethod": "median",
          "assertions": {
            "categories:performance": ["error", { "minScore": 0.95 }],
            "categories:accessibility": ["error", { "minScore": 0.95 }],
            "categories:seo": ["error", { "minScore": 0.95 }],
            "categories:best-practices": ["warn", { "minScore": 0.95 }],
            "largest-contentful-paint": ["error", { "maxNumericValue": 1600 }],
            "total-blocking-time": ["error", { "maxNumericValue": 100 }],
            "cumulative-layout-shift": ["error", { "maxNumericValue": 0.05 }]
          }
        },
```

and **a second entry for the saved-sim page**, which the controller has ruled onto the report page's budget: it mounts the report component set on load exactly as `/reports/<id>` does, and `/reports/<id>` is the one URL in this repository at 200 ms blocking time rather than 100 ms (commit `bf44bdd`, "set the report page blocking-time budget at the 200 ms good threshold"). It is the report page's own entry, verbatim, scoped to a sim id:

```json
        {
          "matchingUrlPattern": ".*/sim/[a-z2-7]{12}\\.html$",
          "aggregationMethod": "median",
          "assertions": {
            "categories:performance": ["error", { "minScore": 0.9 }],
            "categories:accessibility": ["error", { "minScore": 0.95 }],
            "categories:seo": ["error", { "minScore": 0.95 }],
            "categories:best-practices": ["warn", { "minScore": 0.95 }],
            "total-blocking-time": ["error", { "maxNumericValue": 200 }],
            "cumulative-layout-shift": ["error", { "maxNumericValue": 0.05 }]
          }
        },
```

Then widen the first entry's exclusion so **all three** fall out of the catch-all. Today it reads:

```json
          "matchingUrlPattern": "^(?!.*/(?:index|planner|logs)\\.html$)(?!.*/reports/).*$",
```

It needs two more negative lookaheads, not one — `(?!.*/sim(?:/specs)?\.html$)` does not exclude `/sim/simfixtureab.html`, so without the third the saved-sim page would be asserted at 100 ms by the catch-all **and** at 200 ms by its own entry, and lhci applies every matching entry:

```json
          "matchingUrlPattern": "^(?!.*/(?:index|planner|logs)\\.html$)(?!.*/reports/)(?!.*/sim(?:/specs)?\\.html$)(?!.*/sim/[a-z2-7]{12}\\.html$).*$",
```

**`/sim.html` and `/sim/specs.html` keep the site's full budget — performance 0.95, LCP 1600 ms, total blocking time 100 ms — not the planner's and logs' relaxed 0.90.** That is the point of loading the engine after first paint and creating the pool on the first run: the page's own paint is a heading, a panel and four cards, and it must be as fast as a content page. If it is not, the fix is in the page, not the budget. Do not copy the planner's entry, and do not extend the 200 ms entry to either of them.

Verify the three patterns against the three URLs before running lhci, because a lookahead that is one character wrong silently changes which budget a page is held to:

```bash
export PATH=/Users/jh/.nvm/versions/node/v22.12.0/bin:$PATH
cd /Users/jh/code/forever/.worktrees/sim-web/web
node -e "
const cfg = require('./lighthouserc.json');
// The budget each sim URL must land on, and nothing else.
const want = { '/sim.html': 100, '/sim/specs.html': 100, '/sim/simfixtureab.html': 200 };
for (const url of cfg.ci.collect.url) {
  const hit = cfg.ci.assert.assertMatrix
    .filter((m) => new RegExp(m.matchingUrlPattern).test(url))
    .map((m) => m.assertions['total-blocking-time'][1].maxNumericValue);
  console.log(url, '-> TBT', JSON.stringify(hit));
  if (hit.length !== 1) throw new Error(url + ' matches ' + hit.length + ' entries; want exactly 1');
  const path = new URL(url).pathname;
  if (want[path] !== undefined && hit[0] !== want[path]) {
    throw new Error(path + ' resolved to TBT ' + hit[0] + '; want ' + want[path]);
  }
}
console.log('OK');
"
```

Expected, line for line:

```
http://localhost/index.html -> TBT [100]
http://localhost/dungeons/hall-of-thanes.html -> TBT [100]
http://localhost/classes.html -> TBT [100]
http://localhost/planner.html -> TBT [100]
http://localhost/logs.html -> TBT [100]
http://localhost/reports/fixture2abcd.html -> TBT [200]
http://localhost/sim.html -> TBT [100]
http://localhost/sim/specs.html -> TBT [100]
http://localhost/sim/simfixtureab.html -> TBT [200]
OK
```

The **value** check is the point, not just the count. A count-only check passes while the saved-sim page silently falls into the catch-all at 100 ms: that is exactly what happened when the fixture id was `simfixture01`, whose digits `0` and `1` are not in base32's `[a-z2-7]`, so `.*/sim/[a-z2-7]{12}\.html$` never matched it. `simfixtureab` is twelve characters of `[a-z2-7]`, which is what the contract says a `sim_id` is — run this block and read the last two lines before running lhci.

```bash
cd /Users/jh/code/forever/.worktrees/sim-web/web
FOREVER_DATA=fixture npm run build
npm run lhci
```

2. Is `/_sim/` being requested at all on load? It must not be.
3. Is the empty state's grid causing layout shift? Give the four source cards a `min-h` so the grid's height is fixed before the fonts swap.

Do not raise the budget.

# Rulings, and what to put in the finish report

The contract says: "Never change a shape here to resolve a local conflict: rule, record the ruling, and note the friction in the finish report." These are this lane's rulings, as of the 2026-09-18 refresh. Every one of them is implemented in the task named, and every one belongs in the finish report.

| # | The gap | The ruling | Task | Who else it touches |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Armory is not a character source.** Per the contract's `sim-input` row, nothing stores an Armory refresh; the API answers from the newest addon export or last ranked fight. | Three working sources at launch. `fromStoredCharacter` copies whichever source the API reports rather than naming one, so nothing changes here the day Armory lands. The switcher keeps an account card whose copy says where the gear actually comes from, and the landing state carries the same note. **A launch limitation, not a permanent one.** | 7, 11, 18 | api |
| 2 | **The progress payload's shape was unspecified.** The contract said `simRun` "reports progress through the callback" and stopped there. | **Closed, not open.** The contract now pins it at `Pick<SimResult, 'iterations_run' \| 'dps'>`, which `types.ts` mirrors as `SimProgressUpdate`, and engine Task 13 builds `sim/cmd/wasm` to it. No task asks the engine lane to confirm anything, and widening it would need a contract amendment. | 2, 3 | — |
| 3 | **Buff and consumable ids and spell ids were two vocabularies**, and nothing mapped one onto the other. | **Closed on both sides.** The engine lane publishes the accepted list (`sim/request/IDS.md`, generated from the protobuf descriptors), so the web reads it rather than guessing — Task 6 Step 5a checks the launch preset against the file. And the api lane returns `sim-input.buffs` as `IDS.md` ids: `SimInput.buffs` is `string[]`, `fromStoredCharacter` passes them into `CharacterSpec.buffs` unchanged, and **the web never maps a spell id**. A fight's own `COMBATANT_INFO` buffs are still not mapped — `fromLoggedFight` starts on the settings preset — which is the api lane's to close the same way if it becomes worth it. | 2, 6, 7, 12 | api (done) |
| 4 | **`data/curated/specs.json` and `web/src/lib/sim/specs.ts` belong to the data lane.** | **Resolved.** The data lane has merged to `main`: both files are present (27 specs, roles `dps \| tank \| healer`), generated by `python -m pipeline specs`. This lane writes neither and adds no TypeScript generator; it adds only `spec-label.ts`, which joins the class's display name onto the spec's and filters to the dps specs launch scope covers. There is no copy-from-worktree fallback any more — if the files are absent the branch is not rebased far enough. | 1 | — |
| 5 | **A saved sim has no rendered card.** The API draws one per report and per build; a third renderer is not this lane's work. | `/sim/<id>`'s unfurl uses the site's own card, as the rankings and character shells already do. **Deferred to the API lane**, and accepted as such. | 9 | api |
| 6 | **A build's card cannot carry a simmed DPS without a new field.** The design asks for one; the contract adds no field to `BuildDraft` or to the card route. | Saving a build also saves a sim whose `source` is `{ kind: 'build', ref: <build id> }`. The API's card renderer joins on that. No new field on either shape. **Accepted; the API lane owns the card side.** | 21 | api |
| 7 | **There is no rename route for a saved sim.** The design says signed-in players "can name entries"; the contract has `POST /v1/sims` and four reads. | Named at save time, pre-filled with the settings line. No rename. **Accepted as deferred.** | 17 | api |
| 8 | **A stored `SimResult` carries a `CharacterSpec` but not the planner's `Gear` map.** `/sim/<id>` therefore cannot draw the slot grid with icons and rarities without re-fetching the item file for a class it only learns from the result. | The strip says so in one line rather than rendering seventeen `Empty` slots. Drawing it properly is a small follow-up once someone wants it: the item ids are all there in `request.character.gear`. | 17 | — |
| 9 | **`simSplit` and `simCombine` are wasm exports, and the main thread holds no instance.** The design says partials are "combined on the main thread". | The pool routes both to worker 0; `run.ts` still owns what is combined and when, which is the ownership the contract's Web section actually specifies. Loading a second copy of the module on the main thread would defeat the LCP budget for nothing. | 4 | — |
| 10 | **`simAbort` takes one callback id; a pool run is `n` shards.** | Each shard runs under `<callbackId>-<index>` and an abort stops every id with that prefix. The rule lives in exactly two places and a test covers it. | 4 | — |
| 11 | **The engine names nothing.** `sim/adapter/identity.go` emits `spell:25286`, `spell:25286/1`, `spell:25286+r3`, `item:14554`, `other:attack/2` and `unknown`, and the adapter's own comment says "the web resolves a real name from the build's own spells.json". The first draft of this plan assumed display names and would have shipped a damage table of raw keys. | Task 23: one resolver, `resolveActionName`, over a per-class name table pruned at sync time from `spellconst/<class>.json`, the class item list and `spells.json`. `spells.json` whole is 31,754 rows and 1.8 MB, which is more than the engine costs, so it is never fetched by the browser. **No component renders `ability.name` bare**, and there is no numeric `OtherAction` table in `web/` — the engine writes its own enum name into the key, so the resolver only fixes the casing. | 14, 15, 16, 23 | data (`spellconst`, `spells.json`) |
| 12 | **A summary row is keyed by an integer, and two of this lane's tables are joins.** `sim/adapter` (4166eb0) gives every ability, cast and aura a unique `spell_id` — the client id for a plain untagged spell, a derived id at or above **2,000,000** for everything else — and `Summarize` refuses to emit a duplicate (`ErrDuplicateRow`) because a repeated key is a Svelte 5 runtime error in the report's keyed `{#each}` blocks. | Every `{#each}`, map and lookup in this lane keys on `spell_id`, never on a name. Compare mode joins a sim row to a fight row on `spell_id` when it is below `SYNTHETIC_ID_BASE` (a real client id on both sides) and on the resolved display name otherwise; a row matched by neither is its own row with a blank on one side, which is visibly wrong rather than silently wrong. `SYNTHETIC_ID_BASE` is exported from `action-names.ts` with `sim/adapter/identity.go`'s `syntheticBase` named as its source. | 14, 16, 23 | engine |
| 13 | **The engine simulates level 60 and nothing else.** As of 4166eb0 the rule lives only in `api.SimLevel` and `api.SimRequest.Validate`; `request.ErrUnsupportedLevel` is gone. | `CharacterSpec.level` is always `60` and **the sim page offers no level control**. `SimCharacter` keeps a separate `talent_level` for the strip, so a part-levelled build reads honestly ("60 · 24 talent points") instead of sending a level `Validate` will refuse. | 6, 11 | — |
| 14 | **An unknown buff or consumable id fails the whole run, by design.** `sim/request` answers `ErrUnknownBuff`, `ErrUnknownConsume` or `ErrAmbiguousConsume` rather than dropping the id, "because a sim that silently ran without a world buff reports a DPS number that is wrong and says nothing about why". | `SimRunError` carries a `detail` field with the engine's message verbatim, and `RunControl` renders it in mono under the generic failure sentence. **Never paraphrased, never retried without the id.** The launch preset is checked against `IDS.md` before it is written (Task 6 Step 5a), so the default path cannot hit this. | 5, 12, 13 | engine |
| 15 | **A consumable may be a client item id, and resolving it needs a file.** `item:13452` is resolved through `data/builds/<build>/simconsumes.json`, which `request.BuildWith(req, Options{Consumables})` takes; `request.Build` has no table and rejects item ids. | Task 23 publishes `simconsumes.json` to `public/data/<build>/` so the wasm can fetch it, alongside `spells.json` and `spellconst/`. Nothing in `web/` parses it — the web only has to serve it. | 23 | engine, data |
| 16 | **This lane was about to add `msw` beside an idiom the repository already has.** Eight test files stub `globalThis.fetch` with `vi.stubGlobal` and build real `Response`s, and `requestEnvelope` is the one place a browser call to our API is built. | No new dependency. `web/src/test-support/sim-api.ts` is that idiom factored once, with an ordered route table, request recording and a loud throw on an unmatched request. `package.json` is untouched by Task 2. | 2, 7, 11 | — |
| 17 | **`/sim/<sim_id>` mounts the report component set on load, exactly as `/reports/<id>` does**, and `/reports/<id>` is the one URL in `lighthouserc.json` with a 200 ms blocking-time budget rather than 100 ms. | **Ruled, and written.** `/sim.html` and `/sim/specs.html` take the site's full budget — 0.95 performance, 1.6 s LCP, 100 ms TBT — and `/sim/<sim_id>` joins `collect.url` with the report page's entry verbatim (0.90 performance, 200 ms TBT); the contract records it. The catch-all needs **two** new lookaheads, because the sim-shell pattern does not exclude `/sim/<id>` and lhci applies every matching entry — Task 22 Step 6 spells both out and verifies them with a one-liner before lhci runs. Nothing else on this lane moves a budget. | 22 | — |
| 18 | **The engine's throughput is unmeasured in this repository.** Every timing budget in this plan — first estimate within 1 s, final within 8 s, planner live DPS under a second — rests on the design's measured 155 iterations per second per worker, against a fake that returns instantly, and `sim/cmd/wasm` is not built. | The budgets are written into the plan and asserted only where they can be: iteration counts, the 500-iteration live run, the pool size. **The first real `sim.wasm` must be timed against them, and Task 22 re-run, before launch.** | 4, 5, 20, 22 | engine |
| 19 | **Neither a stored character nor a logged fight records a race**, and `CharacterSpec.race` is required — `sim/request.ParseRace` fails without it, and Forever's racials are two actives and two passives per race, so a guess is real damage on the wrong sheet. | **Never `races[0]`.** `fromStoredCharacter` refuses with `simCopy.unknownRace` (the ruling, verbatim); the landing state says so and points at the addon export, which carries the race in its FS1 string, and `SimInput.race` is optional in `types.ts` so the day the API sends it nothing else changes. `fromLoggedFight` cannot refuse — compare mode is a launch feature — so it returns `race_slug: PENDING_RACE` and the strip asks the player once, with the run control disabled until they answer. One click, nothing guessed, and an empty race can never reach a request. | 2, 6, 7, 11, 13, 18 | api, companion |
| 20 | **Aura types disagree in case.** `logs/engine/summary` and the shared `AuraTable` use `'BUFF'`/`'DEBUFF'`; `sim/adapter` wrote `"buff"`. A lower-case type renders an empty Buffs tab, an empty Debuffs tab and a `solo` unfurl on a raid-buffed sim — three silent wrongs. | **The engine side changes**, in its Task 13, which regenerates the adapter goldens anyway. The web keeps `'BUFF'`/`'DEBUFF'` literally and **adds no normaliser**: a case fold in `web/` would be a second vocabulary pretending to be one. Task 2 Step 5 fails the fixture derivation loudly if the copied golden still carries the old form. | 2, 9, 14, 16, 17 | engine |
| 21 | **One in-process worker, written three times.** Tasks 5, 11 and 20 each drive the real pool against the fake engine, and each had its own forty-line copy of the `ToWorker`/`FromWorker` adapter. | `web/src/test-support/fake-worker.ts`, created by Task 3 and imported by all three. One protocol, one adapter; a new message kind is one edit. | 3, 5, 11, 20 | — |

**Resolved since the first draft, and worth saying so in the finish report.** Four things this plan previously worked around are now simply true, and the tasks were rewritten rather than patched:

- The report lane is on `main`, so Task 1 no longer merges a branch, resolves two conflicts or repairs a red `Base.test.ts`. That whole task is gone.
- The data lane owns the spec list and generates `web/src/lib/sim/specs.ts`; this lane no longer writes `data/curated/specs.json` or `web/scripts/sync-specs.mjs`, and `npm run sync` gains no `sync:specs`.
- `ENGINE_VERSION` is real (`df3b36e4c`, `sim/enginever/version.go`) and `version.test.ts` asserts the TypeScript literal against the Go constant, so `make engine-pin` cannot move one without the other.
- The engine lane publishes the buff and consumable id vocabulary (`sim/request/IDS.md`), which is the amendment the first draft's ruling 3 was going to ask for.

**And the one large thing that was already ruled away and still holds:** building `sim.wasm` from the site's own `sim/` module puts `sim/request` and `sim/adapter` inside it, so the web exchanges nothing but JSON — no TypeScript protobuf toolchain, no second copy of the adapter's mapping table. Premium is readable from `GET /v1/me` as `user.premium`.

**Interfaces amendment status.** The controller amended the contract on `main` (`01744f5`) with everything the previous draft of this plan asked for: row identity and naming, the `IDS.md` vocabulary, level 60, the progress payload, the `/sim/<id>` 200 ms budget, and the API mapping character buffs. **Nothing further is requested, and this lane does not edit the contract.** Two small things are worth carrying into the finish report as observations rather than requests:

- `sim-input` still returns no `race`, which is what keeps the account source refusing (ruling 19). The api lane and the companion own it; `SimInput.race` is optional here so the day it lands nothing in `web/` changes.
- `sim/adapter`'s aura type is being changed to `"BUFF"`/`"DEBUFF"` in engine Task 13 (ruling 20). Until that golden is regenerated, Task 2 Step 5's guard stops the fixture from being built at all, which is the intended failure.

## Execution notes

- Task 1 and Task 2 are the base and run first, in that order.
- Then groups **A** (3, 4, 5), **B** (6, 7, 23), **C** (8, 9) and **E** (10) may run at the same time, in four worktrees or four subagents.
- Group **F** (20, 21) may start as soon as A and B are both finished; it does not wait for C, D or E.
- Group **D** (11–19) runs strictly in order after A, B and C — Task 11 loads Task 23's name table into the store, and 14, 15 and 16 all read it from there. **No group-B task edits a group-D file**: Task 23 produces `action-names.ts` and the published `simnames/<class>.json` and stops there.
- Task 22 runs last, alone, and is the only task that runs Lighthouse.
- **No task was removed.** All twenty-two survive with their numbers unchanged, and Task 23 is added. Task 1 kept its number and its place but lost its first three quarters (the `phase-3-web` merge and the spec-list generator), because both jobs are now done elsewhere.
- Repaired in the pre-flight pass, and worth knowing before you start a task: every user-visible string is in `copy.ts` and nowhere else; the in-process worker is one file (`test-support/fake-worker.ts`, Task 3 Step 5) and not three copies; compare mode joins on `spell_id` below `SYNTHETIC_ID_BASE` and on the resolved name above it, never on the name alone; `races[0]` is never a character's race; `premium` lands on `Me['user']` in Task 13, not Task 18; and all three sim URLs are in `collect.url` with two new catch-all lookaheads, not one.
- Removed in the 2026-09-18 refresh, and not to be reinstated: the `phase-3-web` merge (it is on `main`), `data/curated/specs.json` and `web/scripts/sync-specs.mjs` (the data lane's), the `msw` dependency and `web/src/test-support/msw.ts` (the repository has one fetch-stub idiom already), `web/src/lib/sim/shell-paths.ts` (folded into `web/src/lib/report/shell-paths.ts`), and the numeric `OtherAction` table (the engine writes the name itself).
